## 1 Change History
This chapter describes changes not included in the "Parameters", "Counters",
"Glossary", and "Reference Documents" chapters. These changes include:
●  Technical changes
Changes in functions and their corresponding parameters
●  Editorial changes
Improvements or revisions to the documentation
1.1 eRAN18.1 11 (2024-06-21)
This issue includes the following changes.
## Technical Changes
## None
## Editorial Changes
Corrected the formula for calculating the downlink RBLER in the downlink MCS
selection optimization function. For details, see 4.6.7.2.2 Impacts.
Revised parameter descriptions of smart preallocation. For details, see 5.3.5.1
## Principles.
Revised descriptions of the model building duration for data-learning-based uplink
intelligent AMC. For details, see 5.8.4.1 Principles.
Added descriptions of special cases in AMBR-based downlink rate control over
non-GBR services. For details, see 4.4.4.1 Principles.
1.2 eRAN18.1 10 (2023-12-22)
This issue includes the following changes.
## Technical Changes
## None
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description1 Change History
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.1

## Editorial Changes
Revised the conditions for enabling data-learning-based uplink intelligent AMC
when the UMPTg is used as the main control board. For details, see 5.8.4.1
## Principles.
Revised descriptions of the scenarios where uplink traffic-model-based
performance optimization can deliver benefits. For details, see 5.4.4.2.1 Benefits.
Added network impacts of uplink normal preallocation and uplink smart
preallocation. For details, see 5.3.5.2.2 Impacts.
Added descriptions of function impacts of data-learning-based uplink intelligent
AMC. For details, see 5.8.4.2.2 Impacts.
Added descriptions of the impact relationship between UL Multi-Cluster and data-
learning-based uplink intelligent AMC. For details, see 5.7.2.2.2 Impacts.
Revised descriptions of activation verification for data-learning-based uplink
intelligent AMC. For details, see 5.8.4.4.4 Activation Verification.
Revised the MML command examples for data-learning-based uplink intelligent
AMC. For details, see 5.8.4.4.2 Using MML Commands.
1.3 eRAN18.1 09 (2023-09-14)
This issue includes the following changes.
## Technical Changes
## None
## Editorial Changes
Revised descriptions in this document.
Revised the diagram for the downlink scheduling procedure. For details, see 3.2.1
## Downlink Scheduling Procedure.
Revised the setting notes for rate guarantee optimization for downlink GBR and
non-GBR services. For details, see 4.4.8.4.1 Data Preparation.
Revised network monitoring for SRI detection optimization. For details, see
## 5.3.2.4.5 Network Monitoring.
Revised MML command examples for uplink short-interval SPS. For details, see
5.3.9.4.2 Using MML Commands.
Revised MML command examples for uplink scheduling based on the maximum
bit rate. For details, see 5.4.2.4.2 Using MML Commands.
Revised scenario-related descriptions in benefit analysis of downlink frequency
selective scheduling. For details, see 4.5.1.2.1 Benefits.
Revised the setting notes for data-learning-based uplink intelligent AMC. For
details, see 5.8.4.4.1 Data Preparation.

## 2 About This Document
## 2.1 General Statements
## Purpose
This document is intended to acquaint readers with:
●  The technical principles of features and their related parameters
●  The scenarios where these features are used, the benefits they provide, and
the impact they have on networks and functions
●  Requirements of the operating environment that must be met before feature
activation
●  Parameter configuration required for feature activation, verification of feature
activation, and monitoring of feature performance
## NO TE
This document only provides guidance for feature activation. Feature deployment and
feature gains depend on the specifics of the network scenario where the feature is
deployed. To achieve optimal gains, contact Huawei professional service engineers.
Functions mentioned in this document work properly only when enabled in the
specified applicable scenarios (such as RAT and networking). If a function not
mentioned in this document is enabled or a function is enabled in a scenario not
specified as applicable, exceptions or other impacts may occur.
## Software Interfaces
Any parameters, alarms, counters, or managed objects (MOs) described in this
document apply only to the corresponding software release. For future software
releases, refer to the corresponding updated product documentation.
2.2 Applicable RAT
This document applies to FDD.

## 3 General Principles
3.1 Basic Concepts and Definitions
The LTE system uses shared-channel data transmissions where time-frequency
resources are dynamically shared among UEs. eNodeBs perform scheduling to
allocate time-frequency resources for uplink and downlink transmissions.
## Schedulers
LTE schedulers are located at the Media Access Control (MAC) layer. The basic
function of a scheduler is to allocate appropriate system resources to UEs in each
subframe for data transmission and reception.
REs and RBs
Resource elements (REs) are the smallest time-frequency resource units in the LTE
system. An RE occupies one subcarrier in the frequency domain and one
orthogonal frequency division multiplexing (OFDM) symbol in the time domain. A
resource block (RB) includes 12 subcarriers (180 kHz wide), and lasts for one 0.5
ms timeslot. An RB pair forms the minimum scheduling unit, comprising 12
subcarriers in the frequency domain and one subframe (lasting for 1 ms) in the
time domain. Figure 3-1 shows an RB pair.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.16

Figure 3-1 RB pair
A UE can be allocated multiple RBs in each transmission time interval (TTI). The
maximum number of available RBs depends on the system bandwidth. The details
are in Table 3-1.
Table 3-1 Number of available RBs per system bandwidth
System Bandwidth (MHz)
Number of RBs
## 1.46
## 315
## 525
## 1050
## 1575
## 20100

## MCS
Modulation and coding schemes (MCSs) are used by an eNodeB to ensure the
transmission quality and efficiency of UE services. If the channel quality is good,
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.17

an eNodeB selects an MCS with a more efficient modulation scheme and a higher
code rate (with fewer redundant bits inserted). If the channel quality is poor, the
eNodeB selects one with a less efficient modulation scheme and a lower code rate
(with more redundant bits inserted).
The MCS for scheduling can be quadrature phase shift keying (QPSK), 16
quadrature amplitude modulation (16QAM), 64 quadrature amplitude modulation
(64QAM), or 256 quadrature amplitude modulation (256QAM). Each MCS
represents a particular combination of a modulation scheme and a channel code
rate. Among them:
●  QPSK modulates two information bits into each modulation symbol.
●  16QAM modulates four information bits into each modulation symbol.
●  64QAM modulates six information bits into each modulation symbol.
●  256QAM modulates eight information bits into each modulation symbol.
Channel code rates, which are typically less than 1, indicate the proportion of
information bits among physical channel bits after redundancy has been applied.
The scheduler provides a high-order modulation scheme and a high code rate
when channel conditions are favorable. The higher the modulation order and code
rate, the higher the transmission efficiency.
## 3.2 Basic Procedure
Scheduling is the process where the scheduler allocates RBs and selects MCSs for
UEs. In terms of service direction, there are two types of scheduling: uplink and
downlink.
## 3.2.1 Downlink Scheduling Procedure
Figure 3-2 shows the basic process of downlink scheduling. The inputs to the
scheduler include the results of physical-layer measurement and reporting, MAC
protocol data unit (PDU) sending, and upper-layer configuration and UE capability
information processing.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.18

Figure 3-2 Downlink scheduling procedure
Physical-Layer Measurement and Reporting
Physical-layer measurement and reporting involve channel state information (CSI)
and hybrid automatic repeat request (HARQ) feedback.
## ●  CSI
CSI mainly includes the channel quality indicator (CQI) and precoding matrix
indication (PMI). CQI provides the scheduler with the downlink channel
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.19

quality, and PMI offers a preferable precoding matrix in closed-loop spatial
multiplexing transmission mode.
●  HARQ feedback
HARQ feedback indicates whether data has been correctly transmitted or
retransmitted. There are three types of HARQ feedback: acknowledgement
(ACK), negative acknowledgement (NACK), and discontinuous transmission
## (DTX).
MAC PDU Sending
After scheduling a UE and transmitting data, the scheduler calculates and updates
the UE data rate and delay statistics, which are used subsequently to calculate the
scheduling priority for the UE.
Upper-Layer Configuration and UE Capability
Upper-layer configuration mainly refers to the quality of service (QoS)
requirements of UE services. The QoS information about an LTE bearer includes:
●  A QoS class identifier (QCI)
●  A guaranteed bit rate (GBR) of GBR services
●  A maximum bit rate (MBR) of GBR services
●  An aggregate maximum bit rate (AMBR) of non-GBR services
For details on standardized QCIs, see section 6.1.7.2 "Standardized QCI
characteristics" in 3GPP TS 23.203. Huawei eNodeBs also support extended QCIs.
For details, see
QoS Management.
During scheduling, the scheduler considers UE capabilities such as the maximum
number of bits and layers that a UE can transmit in each TTI.
Processing by the Scheduler
In the downlink, after the scheduler obtains required inputs, it first checks
downlink scheduling resources and then performs different types of scheduling in
descending order of scheduling priority: scheduling of control information, semi-
persistent scheduling, HARQ retransmission scheduling, and scheduling of initial
transmission services.
●  For details on control information, see 3.2.1.1 Downlink Control
## Information.
●  For details on semi-persistent scheduling, see
VoLTE.
●  For details on HARQ retransmission, see 3.2.1.2 Downlink HARQ.
●  For details on scheduling of initial transmission services, see 3.2.1.3 Basic
## Downlink Scheduling Policies.
## 3.2.1.1 Downlink Control Information
Downlink control information consists of common control information and
dedicated control information.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.20

## Common Control Information
Downlink common control information includes broadcast messages (such as
system information block 1 [SIB1] and other system information), paging
messages, and random access responses (RARs). Common control information is
reliably transmitted using QPSK and low code rates.
3GPP TS 36.213 defines the following downlink allocation types:
●  Resource allocation type 0
●  Resource allocation type 1
●  Resource allocation type 2
Resource allocation type 2 is used for allocating resources to downlink common
control information. In this allocation type, the allocated resources fall into two
types: distributed virtual resource blocks (DVRBs) and localized virtual resource
blocks (LVRBs).
●  DVRBs are non-contiguous resource blocks. DVRB allocation enhances the
coverage of common control information in the cell. However, the amount of
system resources occupied by common control information increases, which
decreases UE throughput.
●  LVRBs are contiguous resource blocks. LVRB allocation decreases the amount
of system resources occupied by common control information, increasing UE
throughput. However, the coverage of common control information in the cell
is smaller than that when DVRB allocation is used.
Huawei eNodeBs use LVRB allocation.
## Dedicated Control Information
Dedicated control information refers to UE-specific information carried on
signaling radio bearer 0 (SRB0), SRB1, and SRB2. For details about SRB0, SRB1,
and SRB2, see
## Connection Management.
3.2.1.2 Downlink HARQ
## Overview
Huawei eNodeBs use asynchronous adaptive HARQ in the downlink. In this HARQ
scheme, a UE sends HARQ feedback
i TTIs after the initial downlink transmission.
If the feedback is an NACK, the eNodeB retransmits the data at least
k TTIs after
receiving the NACK. In FDD, both i and k are equal to 4.
Downlink HARQ Retransmission Procedure
Figure 3-3 shows the downlink HARQ retransmission procedure in a TTI.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.21

Figure 3-3 Downlink HARQ retransmission procedure
The transport block size (TBS) used for downlink HARQ retransmissions is the
same as the TBS used for initial transmissions. The scheduling priorities of
different HARQ retransmissions are determined by the wait time. A longer wait
time indicates a higher scheduling priority. If all retransmissions have the same
wait time, a retransmission is randomly selected.
MCSs can be adaptively selected for downlink HARQ retransmissions. Due to the
use of combining, HARQ retransmissions can use MCSs with higher indexes than
initial transmissions based on CQI adjustment values.
Downlink HARQ retransmissions for an initial retransmission are stopped when the
number of downlink HARQ retransmissions reaches a maximum value, which is
specified by the CellDlschAlgo.
DlHarqMaxTxNum parameter.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.22

The eNodeB does not perform HARQ retransmissions for a UE in any of the
following scenarios:
●  The UE is in a measurement gap, or enters a measurement gap when it sends
HARQ feedback.
●  The UE is in the sleep time of discontinuous reception (DRX) and the HARQ
operating status is DTX.
●  The UE is not synchronized with the eNodeB or a radio link failure has
occurred.
## 3.2.1.3 Basic Downlink Scheduling Policies
## Basic Downlink Scheduling Algorithms
Four basic downlink scheduling algorithms are available: maximum carrier-to-
interference ratio (Max C/I), round robin (RR), proportional fair (PF), and
enhanced proportional fair (EPF).
The four scheduling algorithms consider different factors in calculating UE
priorities. Details are as follows:
●  Max C/I
The Max C/I algorithm takes only channel quality into account when
allocating air interface resources and schedules only UEs with the best
channel quality at that moment. This algorithm maximizes system
throughput. However, it cannot ensure that UEs in the same cell are
scheduled equally because the UEs in a cell do not all experience the same
channel conditions. A UE may never be scheduled if it is constantly
experiencing poor channel conditions. As a result, user experience is poor. This
scheduling algorithm does not consider QoS for UE services.
## ●  RR
The RR algorithm allows UEs to take turns using shared resources. RR ensures
that UE scheduling is fairer than when Max C/I is used. However, this fairness
is at the expense of system throughput. This scheduling algorithm does not
consider QoS for UE services.
## ●  PF
The PF algorithm is a compromise between Max C/I and RR. This algorithm
takes scheduling fairness, channel quality, and the historical number of bits
transmitted into account. However, this algorithm does not take QoS
requirements into account and therefore cannot ensure satisfactory user
experience.
## ●  EPF
The EPF algorithm is an enhanced version of the PF algorithm. Compared
with PF, EPF considers user experience and guarantees QoS of services. In
addition, Huawei adds six capacity factors for scheduling priority selection
oriented towards capacity or resource fairness in EPF.
Figure 3-4 shows the relationships between the four scheduling algorithms.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.23

Figure 3-4 Relationships between the scheduling algorithms
An operator can select an appropriate scheduling algorithm by setting the
CellDlschAlgo.DlschStrategy parameter. In general, the EPF algorithm is
recommended as the basic downlink scheduling policy.
Scheduling of Initial Transmission Services
When scheduling initial transmission services, the scheduler performs the
following steps:
-  Selects UEs.
The scheduler selects or does not select UEs meeting certain conditions. For
details, see 4.3 UE Selection for Downlink Scheduling.
-  Calculates priorities and manages scheduling queues.
During multi-UE scheduling, the scheduler determines which UEs to schedule
within the specific TTI based on UE priorities. The methods used to calculate
the UE priorities depend on the services. For priority descriptions, see 4.4 UE
Service Priorities in Downlink Scheduling.
-  Determines the amount of data to be scheduled for the UEs.
For downlink scheduling, the scheduler uses the downlink buffer status to
determine the amount.
-  Determines the number of RBs to allocate.
The scheduler estimates the number of RBs to allocate to a UE in the
downlink based on information such as the amount of data to be scheduled
within the specific TTI, number of unused RBs, and remaining power of the
cell. If the estimated number of RBs is not equivalent to an integer number of
resource block groups (RBGs), the scheduler determines a final number of RBs
based on the CellDlschAlgo.
RbgAllocStrategy parameter setting.
-  Allocates RB resources.
The scheduler determines which RBs, among the remaining RBs, to allocate
based on a number of factors. For details, see 4.5 RB Allocation to UEs in
## Downlink Scheduling.
-  Selects MCSs.
The scheduler selects an MCS for a UE mainly based on the UE-reported CQI.
For details on CQI reporting and processing, see
## Channel State Management.
The scheduler maps the CQI to a transport block size index (I
## TBS
) and then
maps the I
## TBS
to an I
## MCS
, which identifies the MCS used for the UE. For details
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.24

about the mapping from I
## TBS
to I
## MCS
, see section 7.1.7 in 3GPP TS 36.213
V12.8.0. In certain scenarios, other information also needs to be considered
for MCS selection. For details, see 4.6 MCS Selection During Downlink
## Scheduling.
## 3.2.2 Uplink Scheduling Procedure
Figure 3-5 shows the basic process of uplink scheduling. The inputs to the
scheduler come from physical-layer measurement and report processing, MAC
PDU reception processing, upper-layer configuration processing, and UE capability
information processing.
Figure 3-5 Uplink scheduling procedure
Physical-Layer Measurement and Reporting
Physical-layer measurement and reporting involve uplink signal to interference
plus noise ratio (SINR) measurement, uplink interference measurement, cyclical
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.25

redundancy check (CRC), and physical uplink shared channel (PUSCH) DTX
detection.
●  Uplink SINR measurement
Uplink SINRs directly reflect the uplink channel quality of UEs. The eNodeB
measures sounding reference signals (SRSs) or demodulation reference signals
(DMRSs) to obtain SINRs.
●  Uplink interference measurement
Uplink interference reflects the amount of interference there is across the
entire bandwidth of a cell. The scheduler considers the interference when it
allocates RB resources to UEs.
## ●  CRC
Uplink data is correctly received only if the CRC results are correct.
●  PUSCH DTX detection
If the CRC fails and DTX is detected on the PUSCH, uplink data is not sent
because no uplink scheduling indication from the eNodeB is received on the
UE side. It is not the case that the UE has sent uplink data but data
demodulation at the eNodeB fails.
MAC PDU Reception
MAC PDU reception includes data reception and MAC control element (CE)
reception.
●  After the scheduler receives uplink data from a UE, the scheduler calculates
and updates the UE data rate, which is used subsequently to calculate the
scheduling priority for the UE.
●  The MAC CEs of a UE mainly include:
–Buffer status reports (BSRs). A UE sends BSRs to the eNodeB to indicate
how much data is in the uplink buffer of the UE. The scheduler allocates
uplink resources to the UE based on the size of this buffer.
–   Power headroom reports (PHRs). A PHR reflects the power status of a UE.
Power headroom is the difference between the maximum allowed
transmit (TX) power and the TX power currently being used in the uplink.
Based on the UE power headroom, the scheduler works with uplink
power control to maintain an appropriate power spectral density for
uplink signals. For details about uplink power control, see
## Power Control.
Upper-Layer Configuration and UE Capability
See Upper-Layer Configuration and UE Capability in 3.2.1 Downlink
## Scheduling Procedure.
Processing by the Scheduler
In uplink scheduling, after the scheduler obtains required inputs, it first checks
uplink scheduling resources and then performs different types of scheduling in
descending order of scheduling priority: semi-persistent scheduling, HARQ
retransmission scheduling, and scheduling of initial transmission services.
●  For details on semi-persistent scheduling, see
VoLTE.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.26

●  For details on HARQ retransmission, see 3.2.2.2 Uplink HARQ.
●  For details on the basic scheduling policies for initial transmission services, see
## 3.2.2.3 Basic Uplink Scheduling Policies.
When scheduling initial transmission services, the scheduler performs the
following steps:
-  The scheduler selects UEs.
The scheduler selects or does not select UEs meeting certain conditions. For
details, see 5.3 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling.
-  The scheduler calculates priorities and manages scheduling queues.
During multi-UE scheduling, the scheduler determines which UEs to schedule
within the specific TTI based on UE priorities. The methods used to calculate
the UE priorities depend on the services. For priority descriptions, see 5.4 UE
Service Priorities in Uplink Scheduling.
-  The scheduler determines the amounts of data to be scheduled for the UEs.
The scheduler triggers uplink scheduling and determines the amounts of data
to schedule based on UE-reported information. For details on the triggering of
uplink scheduling, see 3.2.2.1 Uplink Scheduling Triggering. For details on
the calculation of the amounts of data to schedule, see 5.5 UE Data Amount
Calculation for Uplink Scheduling.
-  The scheduler determines the number of RBs to allocate.
The scheduler estimates the number of uplink RBs required by a UE in the
current TTI based on the buffer status reported by the UE, number of
remaining RBs, power headroom status of the UE, token bucket status for
QoS assurance, and maximum number of RBs supported by a single carrier.
For details on related functions, see 5.6 RB Quantity Calculation for Uplink
## Scheduling.
-  The scheduler allocates RB resources.
The scheduler determines which RBs, among the remaining RBs, to allocate
based on a number of factors. For details, see 5.7 RB Allocation for Uplink
Scheduling. In addition, Huawei eNodeBs support frequency hopping in
accordance with 3GPP specifications. The CellUlschAlgo.
UlHoppingType
parameter is used to configure frequency hopping. However, frequency
hopping has a negative impact on network performance and therefore is not
recommended for use.
-  The scheduler selects MCSs.
The scheduler selects an MCS for a UE mainly based on the uplink SINR. For
details, see 5.8 MCS Selection for Uplink Scheduling.
## 3.2.2.1 Uplink Scheduling Triggering
In the uplink, it is UEs that transmit data. Therefore, the eNodeB needs to know
the uplink buffer status of UEs before it can allocate resources for uplink
scheduling. The procedure for triggering uplink scheduling is as follows:
-  Before transmitting data, a UE sends the eNodeB an SR using the scheduling
request indicator (SRI) on the physical uplink control channel (PUCCH) to
request uplink resources for data transmission.
-  Upon receiving the SR, the eNodeB responds to the SR and schedules the UE.
(This type of UE is referred to as an SR UE in this document.)
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.27

-  The SR UE uses the uplink resources allocated by the eNodeB to transmit
MAC PDUs, including BSRs.
-  If the amount of data indicated by a BSR is greater than 0, the eNodeB
continues scheduling the UE. Otherwise, the eNodeB stops the uplink
scheduling of the UE.
3.2.2.2 Uplink HARQ
## Overview
Synchronous HARQ is used for uplink retransmissions. In this HARQ scheme, the
UE sends uplink data
k TTIs after the eNodeB delivers an uplink grant for the
initial transmission. After i TTIs, the eNodeB sends HARQ feedback to the initial
transmission and then determines whether the UE needs to perform a
retransmission. In FDD, both
i and k are fixed to 4.
Uplink HARQ Retransmission Procedure
There are two types of uplink synchronous HARQ retransmissions: non-adaptive
and adaptive. The differences are as follows:
●  In non-adaptive HARQ, the number of RBs and the MCS used for
retransmissions are identical to those for the initial transmission. If the
positions of RBs for a retransmission collide with those of uplink resources,
such as physical random access channel (PRACH) and PUCCH resources, the
eNodeB re-selects RBs at appropriate positions. If the RB reselection fails, the
eNodeB suspends the retransmission.
●  In adaptive HARQ, the eNodeB adaptively adjusts the number of RBs, their
positions, and the MCS for retransmissions. In this way, uplink resources are
more quickly allocated for uplink retransmissions.
Huawei eNodeBs support both adaptive and non-adaptive HARQ in the uplink and
automatic switching between the adaptive and non-adaptive schemes. The uplink
HARQ retransmission scheme can be set using the
CellUlschAlgo.
AdaptHarqSwitch parameter. Figure 3-6 shows the uplink HARQ
retransmission procedure.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.28

Figure 3-6 Uplink HARQ retransmission procedure
Uplink HARQ retransmissions for an initial retransmission are stopped when the
number of uplink HARQ retransmissions reaches a maximum value, which is
specified by the CellUlschAlgo.
UlHarqMaxTxNum parameter.
## 3.2.2.3 Basic Uplink Scheduling Policies
The basic algorithms in uplink scheduling are Max C/I, RR, PF, and EPF, which are
the same as those in downlink scheduling. An operator can select an appropriate
scheduling algorithm by setting the CellUlschAlgo.
UlschStrategy parameter. In
general, the EPF algorithm is recommended.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.29

For details on each scheduling algorithm and the differences between the
scheduling algorithms, see 3.2.1.3 Basic Downlink Scheduling Policies.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.30

## 4 Downlink Scheduling
## 4.1 Downlink Scheduling Resource Acquisition
Before the scheduler starts downlink scheduling, it needs to obtain available
downlink resources, which mainly refer to RB resources and power resources. This
section describes how manual RB blocking affects RB resources. For details about
power resources, see
## Power Control.
## 4.1.1 Principles
If certain physical downlink shared channel (PDSCH) RBs experience strong
interference from a definite external source or are unavailable because of
spectrum planning, these RBs can be manually blocked so that the eNodeB will
not use them during downlink scheduling.
The parameters in the CellRbReserve managed object (MO) determine the RB
blocking mode and the positions and quantity of these RBs. This RB blocking
function does not take effect on control channels or common channels.

4.2 Optimization of Downlink HARQ Retransmissions
4.2.1 TBS Index Rise for Downlink HARQ Retransmissions
## (FDD)
## 4.2.1.1 Principles
The TBS index can be raised for the first downlink HARQ retransmission to
efficiently utilize soft combining, increasing the retransmission efficiency. This
function is controlled by the PreciseMcsAdaptSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
CqiAdjAlgoSwitch parameter. The amount of the increase is
determined by the CellDlschAlgo.
DlFirstHarqTxTbsIncNum parameter.
## 4.2.1.2 Network Analysis
## 4.2.1.2.1 Benefits
In heavy-load scenarios where the downlink RB usage is greater than 50% and the
downlink initial block error rate (IBLER) is greater than 9% but less than 30%, TBS
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.34

index rise for downlink HARQ retransmissions can efficiently utilize soft
combining, reduce the number of RBs used by retransmissions, and increase User
Downlink Average Throughput. In non-heavy-load scenarios where the
preceding conditions are not met, this function does not bring additional negative
gains to User Downlink Average Throughput.
## 4.2.1.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
●  TBS index rise for downlink HARQ retransmissions lifts the demodulation
threshold, which may cause the downlink residual block error rate (RBLER) to
rise. However, when interference is present, this function reduces the number
of RBs used by retransmissions and therefore lowers overall interference. As a
result, the downlink RBLER may decrease.
●  TBS index rise for downlink HARQ retransmissions reduces RB resources for
retransmissions and uses the saved RBs for initial transmission scheduling.
This may increase the number of initial transmissions and therefore reduce
## Cell Downlink Average Throughput.

4.2.2 TBS Index Reduction for Downlink HARQ
## Retransmissions
## 4.2.2.1 Principles
If consecutive demodulation failures occur during the downlink HARQ
retransmission process, a lower TBS index can be used for HARQ retransmissions
to decrease the demodulation threshold. The TBS used for HARQ retransmissions
is the same as the TBS used for the initial transmission. If the TBS index is reduced,
more RBs need to be allocated for HARQ retransmissions. TBS index reduction for
downlink HARQ retransmissions takes effect when the
DlRetxTbsIndexAdjOptSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
CqiAdjAlgoSwitch
parameter is selected. A reduced TBS index and an increased number of RBs are
used in the last two HARQ retransmissions, after which no HARQ retransmissions
will be allowed because the number of HARQ retransmissions has reached the
maximum.
## 4.2.2.2 Network Analysis
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.37

## 4.2.2.2.1 Benefits
TBS index reduction for downlink HARQ retransmissions can decrease the
downlink RBLER and Service Drop Rate.
## 4.2.2.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
TBS index reduction for downlink HARQ retransmissions requires allocation of
additional RBs and therefore has a slight negative impact on User Downlink
Average Throughput and Cell Downlink Average Throughput.

4.3 UE Selection for Downlink Scheduling
For details on the basic policies used in UE selection for downlink scheduling, see
3.2.1.3 Basic Downlink Scheduling Policies. The scheduler acts differently in UE
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.39

selection for some special scenarios. The UE selection functions used in special
scenarios include the following:
●  Downlink scheduling termination for abnormal UEs
●  PDCCH and PDSCH resource allocation optimization
4.3.1 Downlink Scheduling Termination for Abnormal UEs
## 4.3.1.1 Principles
Termination of the downlink scheduling of UEs in extremely weak coverage areas
or with radio link failures prevents these UEs from overusing system resources. If
the AbnUeSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
CellSchStrategySwitch
parameter is selected, the downlink scheduler evaluates link faults based on the
HARQ feedback transmitted on the PUCCH of format 1a, 1b, or 1bcs. If the HARQ
feedback from a UE indicates a consecutive number of DTXs that exceeds the
value of the CellDlschAlgo.
DlSchAbnUeThd parameter, the downlink scheduler
determines that the UE has encountered a link fault. The scheduler then stops
downlink scheduling for initial transmissions and retransmissions to the UE. If the
UE is working in the carrier aggregation (CA) state, this function takes effect in
the primary cell (PCell) of the UE but does not take effect in any secondary cell
(SCell).
The scheduler resumes downlink scheduling for the UE if any of the following
conditions is met:
●  The PUSCH CRC succeeds once.
●  The CRC for aperiodic CQI reports succeeds once.
●  The eNodeB receives four consecutive CQI reports with non-DTXs over the
## PUCCH.
## 4.3.1.2 Network Analysis
## 4.3.1.2.1 Benefits
Downlink scheduling termination for abnormal UEs (those with links in the
abnormal state) saves system resources, increasing Cell Downlink Average
Throughput and decreasing the downlink RBLER when resources are limited.
## 4.3.1.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
Downlink scheduling termination for abnormal UEs reduces the number of
scheduling opportunities available for UEs under weak coverage and increases
their scheduling delay. This causes User Downlink Average Throughput to
decrease and Service Drop Rate to increase for these UEs.
After downlink scheduling is stopped for abnormal voice UEs, voice packets are
discarded on the base station side because they are not scheduled for a long
period of time. As a result, the downlink voice packet discard rate
((L.PDCP.Tx.Disc.Trf.SDU.QCI.1/L.PDCP.Tx.TotRev.Trf.SDU.QCI.1) x 100%) in the
cell may increase.
eRAN

4.3.2 PDCCH and PDSCH Resource Allocation Optimization
## 4.3.2.1 Principles
When a cell is serving a large number of RRC_CONNECTED UEs with a high
proportion of small-packet data transmission and PDCCH resources are
insufficient, the number of UEs scheduled per TTI in the downlink is limited and
PDSCH resource utilization deteriorates.
In this situation, PDCCH and PDSCH resource allocation can be optimized. With
this optimization, the scheduler reserves PDCCH resources for the last UE to be
scheduled in the downlink, which is always a UE running large-packet services.
The scheduler randomly selects such a UE and allocates the reserved PDCCH
resources to the selected UE. Figure 4-1 illustrates the principles for this
optimization.
Figure 4-1 Principles for PDCCH and PDSCH resource allocation optimization
This optimization involves the following parameters:
●CellDlschAlgo.
DataThdInPdcchPdschBal specifies the data volume threshold
for determining UEs with large-packet data transmission.
●CellDlschAlgo.
UeNumThdInPdcchPdschBal specifies the UE number
threshold for triggering this optimization in a cell.
Note the following when using this optimization:
●  In RAN sharing scenarios, the scheduler selects UEs with large-packet data
transmission only from those served by the high-priority operator in the
current TTI.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.43

●  In CA scenarios, when selecting UEs with large-packet data transmission, the
scheduler considers only the data volume of the bearer on the current carrier.
●  In DRX scenarios, when selecting UEs with large-packet data transmission, the
scheduler considers only UEs in the active time. If a UE enters sleep time, the
eNodeB removes it from the candidate list.
## 4.3.2.2 Network Analysis
## 4.3.2.2.1 Benefits
PDCCH and PDSCH resource allocation optimization increases the downlink RB
usage and therefore increases Cell Downlink Average Throughput.
## 4.3.2.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
●  PDCCH and PDSCH resource allocation optimization increases the downlink
RB usage and downlink interference. This possibly causes Service Drop Rate
to slightly increase.
●  In PDCCH and PDSCH resource allocation optimization, the last UE to be
scheduled in the downlink is always a UE with large-packet data transmission.
Therefore, the scheduling delay of UEs with downlink small-packet data
transmission increases. If the proportion of increase in the downlink
transmission delay is greater than that in the downlink traffic volume, User
Downlink Average Throughput will decrease in the cell.

-  Check that the cell meets the following basic requirements for enabling
PDCCH and PDSCH resource allocation optimization.
–   Average number of UEs in the cell (L.Traffic.User.Avg) > Value of the
CellDlschAlgo.
UeNumThdInPdcchPdschBal parameter
–   CCE usage > 70%, where CCE usage = (L.ChMeas.CCE.CommUsed +
L.ChMeas.CCE.ULUsed + L.ChMeas.CCE.DLUsed)/L.ChMeas.CCE.Avail
–   Downlink RB usage < 80%, where Downlink RB usage =
L.ChMeas.PRB.DL.Used.Avg/L.ChMeas.PRB.DL.Avail
-  Compare the downlink RB usage within the same period of service time
before and after PDCCH and PDSCH resource allocation optimization is
enabled. If the downlink RB usage increases, this function has taken effect.
## 4.3.2.4.5 Network Monitoring
For details, see 4.3.2.2 Network Analysis.
4.4 UE Service Priorities in Downlink Scheduling
The scheduler uses the EPF algorithm to provide end-to-end QoS assurance. This
algorithm is used to calculate the service scheduling priorities and ensure service
rates. UE services can be classified as either GBR services or non-GBR services.
When both GBR and non-GBR services are running in a cell, the scheduler
preferentially guarantees the rates of GBR services, and adopts a best-effort policy
for non-GBR services with a minimum bit rate guaranteed. Scenario-specific
conditions must also be considered for scheduling priority calculation and service
rate guarantee. This section describes the following related functions:
●  Downlink GBR and non-GBR service priority calculation
●  MBR-based downlink rate control
●  Downlink scheduling based on the maximum bit rate
●  AMBR-based downlink rate control over non-GBR services
●  Downlink non-GBR packet bundling
●  Delayed scheduling of downlink radio link control (RLC) status reports
●  Downlink traffic-model-based performance optimization
●  Rate guarantee optimization for downlink GBR and non-GBR services
●  Token bucket-based downlink UE-AMBR control
●  Downlink heavy-load performance optimization (FDD)
4.4.1 Downlink GBR and Non-GBR Service Priority Calculation

## 4.4.1.1 Principles
Non-GBR Service Priority Calculation
The priority of a non-GBR service is determined by factors such as the channel
quality, historical transmission rate, QCI, and weight. The total rate of all non-GBR
services of a UE cannot exceed the per UE aggregate maximum bit rate (UE-
AMBR) set in the evolved packet core (EPC). The priority of a non-GBR service is
calculated using the following formula:
where
## ●
indicates the current channel quality of the UE.
## ●
indicates the capacity factor for EPF. This factor can be set using the
CellDlschAlgo.
DlEpfCapacityFactor parameter:
–   When the factor is set to 1, the scheduling priority selection for non-GBR
services is oriented towards resource fairness.
–   When the factor is set to a value smaller than 1, the scheduling priority
selection for non-GBR services is oriented towards rate fairness.
–   When the factor is set to a value greater than 1, the scheduling priority
selection for non-GBR services is oriented towards capacity.
## ●
indicates the historical transmission rate of the UE. The calculation of this
rate is dependent on the setting of the EpfEnhancedSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter.
–   When the EpfEnhancedSwitch option is deselected,  indicates the
average scheduled rate, which the scheduler obtains through filtering.
–   If the EpfEnhancedSwitch option is selected,
indicates the result of
dividing the transmitted data volume by the data transmission duration.
If inter-BBP CA is enabled, the
CellDlschAlgo.
EpfEnhancedSchOptSwitch parameter needs to be set to
ON. This enables the eNodeB to calculate the scheduling priorities of UEs
in their SCells based on the historical transmission rates sent from their
respective PCells.
## ●
indicates the weight factor for the scheduling priority corresponding to
the QCI of the non-GBR service. This factor can be set using the
CellQciParaExtension.
DlSchPriorityWeightFactor parameter. A larger weight
factor indicates a higher scheduling priority.
● indicates the weight coefficient for the downlink scheduling priority
corresponding to the subscriber profile ID (SPID) of the UE. This weight
coefficient is specified by the SpidCfg.
DlSchPriCoeff parameter and is valid
only when the eNodeB identifies that the UE is configured with an SPID. For
details, see
## WBB.
● indicates the delay for the non-GBR service packets to wait for
scheduling on the eNodeB side. The downlink packet bundling switch controls

whether this delay is considered in scheduling priority calculation. For details,

GBR Service Priority Calculation
The eNodeB maximizes spectral efficiency while ensuring that the bit rates of GBR
services are not lower than their GBRs. Therefore, the priority of a GBR service is
calculated based on the channel quality and service delay of a UE by using the
following formula:
where
indicates the channel quality.
indicates the packet delay budget (PDB) of the GBR service.
Resource Proportions for GBR and Non-GBR Services
When both GBR and non-GBR services are running on a network, schedulers
preferentially guarantee GBR services, which generally have a higher priority than
non-GBR services. If insufficient radio resources are available in a cell that has a
large proportion of GBR services, non-GBR services may not be scheduled. To
prevent this:
●  The CellDlschAlgo.
NonGbrResourceRatio parameter can be used to specify
the proportion of resources reserved for non-GBR services.
●  The CellQciParaExtension.
DlMinGbr parameter can be used to specify the
minimum guaranteed rate of non-GBR services with the specified QCI.
## 4.4.1.2 Network Analysis
## 4.4.1.2.1 Benefits
GBR and non-GBR service priority calculation guarantees the QoS for services of
various QCIs and provides differentiated services.
When the EpfEnhancedSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.DlSchSwitch
parameter is selected, the value of User Downlink Average Throughput
increases.

4.4.2 MBR-based Downlink Rate Control
## 4.4.2.1 Principles
The MBR is a QoS parameter set in the EPC for GBR services. Generally, the MBR
must be greater than or equal to the GBR. When the scheduler schedules GBR
services, it determines whether to consider the MBR for downlink rate control
based on the setting of the DlMbrCtrlSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter. If the MBR configured on the EPC is less
than the GBR, the eNodeB uses the MBR as the GBR for QoS management.
●  When the DlMbrCtrlSwitch option is deselected, the scheduler preferentially
guarantees GBR services in a way that their data rates do not exceed the GBR.
The priority calculation method for such GBR services is the same as that
described in GBR Service Priority Calculation.
●  When the DlMbrCtrlSwitch option is selected, the scheduler considers MBR-
based downlink rate control. In this situation, the rates of GBR services can
exceed the GBR, but not the MBR. When the data rate of a GBR service
exceeds the GBR, this GBR service is scheduled together with non-GBR
services. Therefore, priority calculation for this GBR service is the same as that
for non-GBR services (described in Non-GBR Service Priority Calculation).
## 4.4.2.2 Network Analysis
## 4.4.2.2.1 Benefits
If the DlMbrCtrlSwitch option is selected, GBR services can be allocated more
resources. The average downlink throughput of GBR services increases and can
exceed the GBR, with the MBR as the upper limit.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.53

## 4.4.2.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
GBR services use more resources when the network is congested, which reduces
the amount of resources available for non-GBR services. As a result, the average
downlink service throughput on the non-GBR bearers declines.

## 4.4.2.4.4 Activation Verification
-  Enable downlink GBR services for a UE in a 20 MHz cell. Set the GBR and
MBR to 10 Mbit/s and 20 Mbit/s, respectively. Use the UE to initiate a service
with a downlink data rate greater than the MBR.
-  On the MAE-Access, start throughput monitoring (corresponding to
Throughput Monitoring on the GUI) and cell RB usage monitoring
(corresponding to Usage of RB Monitoring on the GUI).
-  Compare GBR service rates delivered before and after the function switch is
turned on, assuming that the cell RBs are sufficient. When the switch is
turned off, the GBR service rate is approximately 10 Mbit/s (GBR). When the
switch is turned on, the GBR service rate is greater than or equal to the GBR
but less than the MBR.
eRAN

## 4.4.2.4.5 Network Monitoring
Use the counters L.Thrp.DL.BitRate.Samp.Index0 to
L.Thrp.DL.BitRate.Samp.Index9 to monitor the downlink UE throughput
distribution. After this function is enabled, the downlink UE throughput
distribution indicates a general throughput change towards higher throughput
ranges.
4.4.3 Downlink Scheduling Based on the Maximum Bit Rate
## 4.4.3.1 Principles
In the EPC, a UE-AMBR and an MBR are set for a UE. The total rate of all
downlink non-GBR services running on the UE cannot exceed the UE-AMBR, and
the total rate of all GBR services running on the UE cannot exceed the MBR.
When different UEs in a cell have different UE-AMBRs:
●  If the UeAmbrDlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch
parameter is deselected, the eNodeB does not consider UE-AMBRs when
calculating scheduling priorities.
●  If the UeAmbrDlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch
parameter is selected, the eNodeB considers UE-AMBRs when calculating
scheduling priorities to implement differentiated scheduling of non-GBR
services with different service rate subscriptions.
When different UEs in a cell have different MBRs:
●  If the MbrDlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter
is deselected, the eNodeB does not consider MBRs when calculating
scheduling priorities.
●  If the MbrDlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter
is selected, the eNodeB considers MBRs when calculating scheduling priorities
to implement differentiated scheduling of GBR services with different service
rate subscriptions.
## 4.4.3.2 Network Analysis
## 4.4.3.2.1 Benefits
●  When the UeAmbrDlSchSwitch option is selected, resources are preferentially
allocated for UEs with high UE-AMBRs. The average downlink UE throughput
increases for such UEs.
●  When the MbrDlSchSwitch option is selected, resources are preferentially
allocated for UEs with high MBRs. The average downlink UE throughput
increases for such UEs.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.56

## 4.4.3.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
●  If the UeAmbrDlSchSwitch option is selected when the system is congested,
a large amount of resources will be occupied by UEs with high UE-AMBRs.
This may cause Cell Downlink Average Throughput to decrease.
●  If the MbrDlSchSwitch option is selected when the system is congested, a
large amount of resources will be occupied by UEs with high MBRs. This may
cause Cell Downlink Average Throughput to decrease.

## 4.4.3.4.4 Activation Verification
-  Enable UE 1, UE 2, and UE 3 with the same channel conditions and an AMBR
of 30 Mbit/s, 100 Mbit/s, and 100 Mbit/s, respectively, to access a 10 MHz cell
from the cell center and have non-GBR bearers established.
-  Use the three UEs to perform downlink full buffer services at the same time,
with the services carried on the non-GBR bearers. Ensure that the actual rate
of each UE is lower than the AMBR of the UE.
-  Check the Code0 DL Used RB Num values for the three UEs on the MAE-
Access to obtain the number of RBs used by each UE before and after the
UeAmbrDlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter
is selected. Before this option is selected, the percentage of the numbers of
RBs used by each UE is almost the same. If the percentage of RBs used by UE
1 decreases after this option is selected, this function has taken effect.
-  Verify the function activation on UEs running GBR services in a similar way.
## 4.4.3.4.5 Network Monitoring
Use the counters L.Thrp.DL.BitRate.Samp.Index0 to
L.Thrp.DL.BitRate.Samp.Index9 to monitor the downlink UE throughput
distribution.
4.4.4 AMBR-based Downlink Rate Control Over Non-GBR
## Services
## 4.4.4.1 Principles
To ensure fairness between UEs, it is recommended that UE-AMBR-based rate
control be used on the eNodeB side. With this function, the eNodeB ensures that
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.59

the total number of non-GBR service bits sent to a UE within a period does not
exceed the UE-AMBR multiplied by the period. This period is specified by the
CellDlschAlgo.AmbrCtrlTcycle parameter. This parameter can be set to larger
values if network resources are sufficient and an operator accepts less precise
control or does not plan to use UE-AMBR-based rate control. Figure 4-2 illustrates
this function.
Figure 4-2 Principles of downlink AMBR-based rate control over non-GBR services
If the VoiceDataDifferentiationSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
ServiceDiffSwitch parameter is selected, the control does not
take effect on QCI-5 services in downlink scheduling. The scheduler continues
scheduling resources for QCI-5 services of a UE even if the total number of bits
sent to the UE within a period has reached the UE-AMBR multiplied by the period.
## NO TE
AMBR-based downlink rate control over non-GBR services may not accurately limit the
rates of UEs due to factors such as RLC status reporting, multi-carrier CA UE distribution,
and coexistence of multiple UEs in a cell.
## 4.4.4.2 Network Analysis
## 4.4.4.2.1 Benefits
A larger value of the CellDlschAlgo.AmbrCtrlTcycle parameter results in more
data being scheduled in a single instance within a period. This reduces the wait
time during scheduling and improves User Downlink Average Throughput for
UEs with low UE-AMBRs.
## 4.4.4.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
If the CellDlschAlgo.
AmbrCtrlTcycle parameter is set to a large value, single-UE
TCP packet injection may cause the TCP send window to become unstable. As a
result, the incoming traffic volume is insufficient and User Downlink Average
Throughput decreases.

4.4.5 Downlink Non-GBR Packet Bundling
## 4.4.5.1 Principles
When calculating the scheduling priority of a non-GBR service as described in
Non-GBR Service Priority Calculation, the eNodeB may consider the scheduling
wait time of non-GBR packets on the eNodeB side, depending on the
NonGbrBundlingSwitch option setting of the CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch
parameter.
●  If this option is deselected, the eNodeB does not consider the wait time.
●  If this option is selected, the eNodeB considers the wait time.
## 4.4.5.2 Network Analysis
## 4.4.5.2.1 Benefits
When the control channel is congested, bundling multiple downlink non-GBR
service packets before transmission increases control channel resource utilization,
and improves the transmission delay distribution of non-GBR service packets with
an increased proportion of low-delay transmissions.
## 4.4.5.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
When control channel resources are sufficient or traffic channel resources are
insufficient in the downlink in a cell, and there are a large number of UEs running
large-packet services, downlink non-GBR packet bundling allows these UEs to be
preferentially scheduled due to their long scheduling wait time and therefore
preempt scheduling opportunities of UEs running small-packet services. When the
UEs running large-packet services are located at the cell edge, they consume more
scheduling resources in the cell. As a result, User Downlink Average Throughput
decreases.
eRAN

## 4.4.5.4.4 Activation Verification
This section uses a 10 MHz cell with one PDCCH symbol as an example.
-  Use six UEs to attach to the cell from the cell center, and move them to the
cell edge. Then, inject downlink packets of 100 bytes each to each UE at 500
kbit/s.
-  On the MAE-Access, start the following monitoring tasks for the six UEs: MCS-
based count monitoring (corresponding to MCS Count Monitoring on the
GUI), cell DCI monitoring (corresponding to DCI Statistic Monitoring on the
GUI), cell RB usage monitoring (corresponding to Usage of RB Monitoring
on the GUI), and cell data radio bearer (DRB) monitoring (corresponding to
DRB Statistic Monitoring on the GUI).
-  Adjust the signal quality of the UEs to achieve the following:
–   The UEs are scheduled in the downlink with an MCS index of about 7.
–   The sum of the uplink DCI usage (specified by UL DCI Utilization Within
a Monitoring Period) and the downlink DCI usage (specified by DL DCI
Utilization Within a Monitoring Period) for these UEs is greater than
## 80%.
–   The downlink cell RB usage is between 60% and 80%.
-  In cell DRB monitoring, check the total number of data packets and the total
transmission delay for multiple 2-minute periods. Uses the 2-minute data
samples to calculate the average delay and variance.
-  Enable the bundling function, and calculate the average delay. Check that the
average delay and variance decrease.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.66

## 4.4.5.4.5 Network Monitoring
Observe the average transmission delay of downlink non-GBR packets by using
the following formula: Sum (L.Traffic.DL.PktDelay.Time.QCI.6 to
L.Traffic.DL.PktDelay.Time.QCI.9)/Sum (L.Traffic.DL.PktDelay.Num.QCI.6 to
L.Traffic.DL.PktDelay.Num.QCI.9).
4.4.6 Delayed Scheduling of Downlink RLC Status Reports
## 4.4.6.1 Principles
This function is used when PDCCH CCE resources are insufficient during major
incidents, for example, when the RB usage, the number of RRC_CONNECTED UEs,
and the PDCCH CCE usage are greater than 80%, 200, and 70%, respectively.
Certain RLC ACK status reports, of which the wait time has not elapsed, are not
transmitted until there is downlink packet bundling. In normal situations, these
reports are separately transmitted. Figure 4-3 illustrates the principles for this
function. This function is controlled by the DlRLCStateReportSchDelaySw option
of the CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter.
Figure 4-3 Principles for delayed scheduling of downlink RLC status reports
## 4.4.6.2 Network Analysis
## 4.4.6.2.1 Benefits
When PDCCH CCE resources are insufficient, if the scheduling of downlink RLC
status reports is delayed, User Downlink Average Throughput is increased. When
PDCCH CCE resources are sufficient, this function is less likely to take effect. After
this function takes effect, it does not bring additional negative gains.

4.4.7 Downlink Traffic-Model-based Performance
## Optimization
## 4.4.7.1 Principles
Given the traffic model difference among UEs on live networks, the scheduler can
consider both the traffic model and signal quality during scheduling to
preferentially schedule UEs with smaller amount of service buffer data and better
signal quality. This function is controlled by the DlPacketLenAwareSchSw option
of the CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter.
## 4.4.7.2 Network Analysis
## 4.4.7.2.1 Benefits
In scenarios with a heavy traffic load (for example, an RB usage greater than
60%), downlink traffic-model-based performance optimization reduces scheduling
wait time for UEs, and increases User Downlink Average Throughput by 5% to
20% while basically maintaining cell capacity. In scenarios with a light traffic load,
this function increases User Downlink Average Throughput less significantly but
does not bring additional negative gains.
## 4.4.7.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
In heavy-load scenarios (for example, downlink RB usage higher than 80%), the
values of the counters L.Traffic.DL.PktDelay.Time.QCI.1 to
L.Traffic.DL.PktDelay.Time.QCI.9 might increase.

small-packet services be 1:4.
Step 3Check the average downlink UE throughput before and after enabling this

for a period of time, this function has taken effect.

4.4.8 Rate Guarantee Optimization for Downlink GBR and
Non-GBR Services
## 4.4.8.1 Principles
In heavy-load scenarios, the scheduling priorities are not updated promptly,
possibly making it difficult to reach the GBR configured for GBR services or the
minimum GBR (specified by CellQciParaExtension.
DlMinGbr) configured for non-
GBR services. Rate guarantee optimization for downlink GBR and non-GBR services
can be enabled using the MIN_GBR_OPT_SWITCH option of the
CellAlgoExtSwitch.
DlSchEnhSwitch parameter. This function enables the eNodeB
to update UE scheduling priorities in a more timely manner in heavy-load
scenarios, ensuring the downlink rate of UEs configured with the GBR or the
CellQciParaExtension.
DlMinGbr parameter.
## 4.4.8.2 Network Analysis
## 4.4.8.2.1 Benefits
In heavy-load scenarios (for example, when the number of UEs on a single BBP is
greater than 400), rate guarantee optimization for downlink GBR and non-GBR
services increases User Downlink Average Throughput of GBR or non-GBR
services to be guaranteed.

4.4.9 Token Bucket-based Downlink UE-AMBR Control
## 4.4.9.1 Principles
In 4.4.4 AMBR-based Downlink Rate Control Over Non-GBR Services, a method
for periodically controlling the downlink UE-AMBR is introduced. However, this
method does not resolve the following two issues:
●  The rate of a UE remains strictly limited not to exceed UE-AMBR within the
current control period, even if the UE has not been scheduled for a long time.
This is because the preceding method does not consider historical scheduling.
●  The rate of a UE remains strictly limited based on the UE-AMBR, even if the
cell's RB resources are completely idle. This impedes full utilization of RB
resources and improvement of user experience.
For these reasons, a downlink UE-AMBR rate control method is added to the
eNodeB based on the token bucket mechanism in Annex B of 3GPP TS 23.107
V16.0. Figure 4-4 shows the basic principle.
Figure 4-4 Token bucket-based downlink UE-AMBR control
Each UE maintains a UE-AMBR token bucket, into which it periodically injects
tokens. The rate at which tokens are injected into and removed from the token
bucket is the downlink UE-AMBR and the downlink scheduling rate, respectively. If
the scheduling rate is greater than the downlink UE-AMBR, more tokens are
removed from the bucket than are injected into it, eventually leading to a lack of
tokens and the scheduler no longer being able to schedule the UE. At the early
scheduling stage of new UE packets, a large number of tokens may exist in the
token bucket, meaning that the instantaneous scheduling rate may exceed the UE-
## AMBR.
Token bucket-based downlink UE-AMBR control includes basic and optimization
functions, which are controlled by the CellDlSchExtParam.
DlMaxAmbrToken and
CellDlSchExtParam.
DlMinAmbrToken parameters, respectively. The basic
function takes effect only for newly admitted UEs, and the optimization function
takes effect only when the basic function has taken effect.
●  For the basic function:
–   When the CellDlSchExtParam.
DlMaxAmbrToken parameter is set to 0,
the basic function of token bucket-based downlink UE-AMBR control
does not take effect.
–   When the CellDlSchExtParam.
DlMaxAmbrToken parameter is set to a
value greater than 0, the basic function of token bucket-based downlink
UE-AMBR control takes effect. The maximum number of tokens that can
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.77

be contained in the token bucket is calculated as follows:
CellDlSchExtParam.DlMaxAmbrToken x UE-AMBR. If the basic function
takes effect and the number of remaining tokens in the bucket is less
than 0, the eNodeB stops scheduling the UE. The eNodeB resumes
downlink scheduling only when the number of remaining tokens in the
bucket is greater than or equal to 0.
●  For the optimization function:
–   When the CellDlSchExtParam.
DlMinAmbrToken parameter is set to 0,
the optimization function of token bucket-based downlink UE-AMBR
control does not take effect.
–   When the CellDlSchExtParam.
DlMinAmbrToken parameter is set to a
value greater than 0, the optimization function of token bucket-based
downlink UE-AMBR control takes effect. The number of remaining tokens
in the downlink UE-AMBR token bucket can be a negative value and the
minimum value is calculated as follows: Minimum value = 0 –
(CellDlSchExtParam.
DlMinAmbrToken x UE-AMBR). If the optimization
function takes effect and the number of remaining tokens in the bucket is
less than 0 but greater than or equal to the configured minimum value,
the UE can still be scheduled, but the scheduling priority decreases. The
eNodeB stops scheduling the UE when the number of tokens in the
bucket is less than the minimum value.
## 4.4.9.2 Network Analysis
## 4.4.9.2.1 Benefits
Token bucket-based downlink UE-AMBR control provides gains when all of the
following conditions are met:
●  The burst traffic model is mainly used. For example, the proportion of last
TTIs is greater than 20%.
●  There are a large number of UEs with a low UE-AMBR in the cell. For
example, the proportion of UEs with a UE-AMBR lower than 5 Mbit/s is
greater than 20%.
When the preceding conditions are met, the basic function of token bucket-based
downlink UE-AMBR control can increase User Downlink Average Throughput by
up to 5%. If the optimization function also takes effect, User Downlink Average
Throughput can be further increased by up to 5%.
## 4.4.9.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
Enabling token bucket-based downlink UE-AMBR control on a network with many
low-AMBR UEs increases the rates of these UEs. However, the traffic volume of
these UEs may increase, leading to increased downlink PRB usage and CCE usage
of the cell. If the traffic increase is mainly from UEs performing small-packet
services or UEs at the cell edge, the downlink cell throughput and downlink
spectral efficiency decrease. Conversely, if the traffic increase is mainly from UEs
performing large-packet services or UEs near the cell center, the downlink cell
throughput and downlink spectral efficiency increase.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.78

When the load is heavy (for example, the downlink PRB usage is greater than
40%), the traffic increase of low-AMBR UEs may occupy resources of high-AMBR
UEs. As a result, the downlink throughput of high-AMBR UEs decreases by no
more than 5%.

4.4.10 Downlink Heavy-Load Performance Optimization (FDD)
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.81

## 4.4.10.1 Principles
In downlink heavy load scenarios, the user-perceived rate of UEs in a cell
decreases as the number of UEs increases. The downlink heavy-load performance
optimization function automatically checks for UEs performing large-packet
services in the cell and increases the downlink user-perceived rate of such UEs.
This function is controlled by the reserved parameter
eNBCellRsvdPara.
RsvdU32Para13. For details about this reserved parameter, see
## 6 Parameters.
●  If the reserved parameter eNBCellRsvdPara.
RsvdU32Para13 is set to a value
in the range of 5 to 20, this function takes effect. If the downlink data volume
of a UE is greater than or equal to this threshold, this UE is identified as a
large-packet-service UE for downlink heavy-load performance optimization,
and the downlink user-perceived rate of this UE is increased.
●  If the reserved parameter eNBCellRsvdPara.
RsvdU32Para13 is set to a value
outside the range of 5 to 20, this function is disabled.
## 4.4.10.2 Network Analysis
## 4.4.10.2.1 Benefits
Downlink heavy-load performance optimization increases the value of User
Downlink Average Throughput for UEs performing large-packet services.
## 4.4.10.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
●  A smaller value of the reserved parameter eNBCellRsvdPara.RsvdU32Para13
indicates a higher proportion of UEs that are detected as performing large-
packet services in a cell and lower improvement in the experience of such
UEs.
●  Downlink heavy-load performance optimization may increase the CPU usage
of the main control board and BBP.

There are no license requirements for basic functions.
