4.5 RB Allocation to UEs in Downlink Scheduling
## 4.5.1 Downlink Frequency Selective Scheduling
## 4.5.1.1 Principles
Frequency selective scheduling and frequency diversity scheduling are two basic
downlink RB allocation modes. Frequency diversity scheduling is used by default.
## Frequency Diversity Scheduling
Frequency diversity scheduling does not consider the differences in the frequency-
domain channel quality for UEs. The eNodeB calculates the priorities of wideband
scheduling and selects MCSs for downlink transmissions based on wideband CQIs
reported by UEs. Based on the priority calculation results, the eNodeB allocates
downlink RBs to the UEs from a low frequency band to a high frequency band, as
shown in Figure 4-5.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.84

Figure 4-5 Downlink resource allocation in frequency diversity scheduling
## Frequency Selective Scheduling
Frequency selective scheduling leverages the differences in the frequency-domain
channel quality for UEs to produce gains. The eNodeB calculates the subband-
specific scheduling priorities of UEs based on sub-band CQIs reported by the UEs
and schedules the UEs based on these priorities so that the UEs can be allocated
the resources in their respective sub-bands with the optimum channel quality. As
shown in Figure 4-6, RBGs a and c are allocated to UE 1 and RBG b is allocated to
## UE 2.
Figure 4-6 Downlink resource allocation in frequency selective scheduling
Downlink frequency selective scheduling includes the following functions:
●  Basic frequency selective scheduling
●  Smart frequency selective scheduling
●  Two-dimensional frequency selective scheduling
Figure 4-7 shows the relationship between basic frequency selective scheduling
and smart frequency selective scheduling.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.85

Figure 4-7 Relationship between basic frequency selective scheduling and smart
frequency selective scheduling
Two-dimensional frequency selective scheduling, supported only by FDD, has
basically the same scheduling framework as smart frequency selective scheduling
and makes optimizations. Therefore, the relationship between two-dimensional
frequency selective scheduling and basic frequency selective scheduling is similar
to that shown in Figure 4-7.
## Basic Frequency Selective Scheduling
Basic frequency selective scheduling is specified by the FreqSelSwitch option of
the CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter. The main procedures include target
UE selection and frequency selective scheduling.
-  Target UE selection
The eNodeB periodically checks channel fluctuations and the moving speed of
each UE. When a UE meets specific conditions, downlink frequency selective
scheduling is triggered. When the conditions are not met, downlink frequency
selective scheduling stops. The check period is specified by the
CellDlschAlgo.
FreqSelJudgePeriod parameter. Setting this parameter to a
small value accelerates triggering downlink frequency selective scheduling for
UEs that meet specific conditions and boosts the improvement in downlink
spectral efficiency. However, more aperiodic CQI reports are required to
trigger downlink frequency selective scheduling, increasing interference to
uplink transmissions.
Channel fluctuation of UEs is contained in the subband CQI information that
can be derived from aperiodic CQI reporting. The moving speeds of UEs are
obtained through Doppler measurements on the eNodeB side.
–   Aperiodic CQI reporting
The triggering period and validity period of aperiodic CQI reporting are
specified by the CellDlschAlgo.
FSUEAperCQITrigPeriod and
CellDlschAlgo.
FSUESbCQIValidityPeriod parameters, respectively. If
there are UE compatibility issues, it is recommended that the
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.86

ApCqiAndAckAbnCtrlSwitch option of the
ENodeBAlgoSwitch.CompatibilityCtrlSwitch parameter be selected.
For details about aperiodic CQIs, see
## Channel State Management.
–   Doppler measurement
Frequency selective scheduling is mainly used in low-speed scenarios. The
eNodeB can estimate the moving speed of a UE based on Doppler shifts.
The FreqSelJudgeIgnorDopplerSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter specifies whether frequency
selective scheduling considers Doppler measurement results.
▪If this option is selected, downlink frequency selective scheduling
does not consider Doppler measurement results. This setting can be
used for hotspots where UEs move at low speeds, reducing the
negative impact of inaccurate Doppler measurements.
▪If this option is deselected, downlink frequency selective scheduling
considers Doppler measurement results. This setting applies to
common cells.
Without considering Doppler measurement results, frequency selective
scheduling may be triggered for medium- or high-speed UEs even when
Doppler conditions are not met.
The eNodeB also supports Doppler measurement level selection and
Doppler measurement reporting period selection. The Doppler
measurement level is specified by the CellUlschAlgo.
DopMeasLevel
parameter.
## ▪
If this parameter is set to CLASS_0, the eNodeB performs Doppler
measurements on PUSCH DMRSs with the SINR being 5 dB or higher.
## ▪
If this parameter is set to CLASS_1, the eNodeB performs Doppler
measurements on PUSCH DMRSs with the SINR being –5 dB or
higher.
The Doppler measurement reporting period selection is specified by the
FastReportDopSwitch option of the CellUlschAlgo.
DopAlgoSwitch
parameter.
## ▪
If this option is selected, a short Doppler measurement reporting
period is used. This setting is recommended when a short frequency
selective scheduling evaluation period is configured.
▪If this option is deselected, the normal Doppler measurement
reporting period is used.
-  Frequency selective scheduling
The eNodeB calculates the scheduling priorities and selects downlink MCS
indexes based on subband CQIs for target UEs on each subband, and based
on full-band CQIs for non-target UEs. The eNodeB then schedules UEs on
each subband based on the scheduling priorities and allocates downlink RBs
from the low frequency band to the high frequency band in sequence.
The CellPdcchAlgo.
PdcchSymNumSwitch parameter setting and the number
of occupied symbols affect the triggering of frequency selective scheduling, as
described in Table 4-18.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.87

Table 4-18 Impact of the number of PDCCH symbols on the triggering of
frequency selective scheduling
PdcchSymNumSwitchNumber of PDCCH SymbolsWhether
to Trigger
## Frequency
## Selective
## Scheduling
ONMaximum number of PDCCH
symbols for a period
## Yes
OtherNo
ECFIADAPTIONONAny adaptively assigned numberYes
OFFAny permanently assigned numberYes

## Smart Frequency Selective Scheduling
Smart frequency selective scheduling is controlled by the SMART_FSS_SWITCH
option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchExtSwitch parameter. Before this option is
selected, the FreqSelSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.DlSchSwitch
parameter must be selected. The main procedures include target UE selection, cell
frequency selective status determination, and frequency selective scheduling.
-  Target UE selection
In addition to aperiodic CQI reporting and Doppler measurement that are
required in basic frequency scheduling, target UE selection for smart
frequency selective scheduling requires large-packet UE identification.
–   Aperiodic CQI reporting
Unlike basic frequency selective scheduling, smart frequency selective
scheduling enables the eNodeB to adjust the triggering period and
validity period for aperiodic CQI reporting dynamically, instead of using
fixed parameter configurations. In addition, the
APERIODIC_CQI_RPT_OPT_SW option of the
CellAlgoExtSwitch.
DlSchEnhSwitch parameter can be selected. This
allows UEs engaged in downlink smart frequency selective scheduling to
send aperiodic CQI reports in a more timely manner and prolongs the
validity period of these CQI reports, increasing the proportion of UEs with
downlink smart frequency selective scheduling in effect.
–   Doppler measurement
Smart frequency selective scheduling processes Doppler measurements in
the same way as basic frequency selective scheduling.
## NO TE
When smart frequency selective scheduling is in effect, the eNodeB still considers
Doppler shifts when determining whether to stop smart frequency selective
scheduling for UEs even if the FreqSelJudgeIgnorDopplerSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter is selected.
–   Large-packet UE identification
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.88

If the downlink data volume of a UE is greater than or equal to the value
of the CellDlschAlgo.DlUserDataThld parameter, the UE is considered as
a large-packet UE. Smart frequency selective scheduling is performed
only for large-packet UEs.
-  Cell frequency selective status determination
Compared with basic frequency selective scheduling, smart frequency selective
scheduling additionally determines the cell frequency selective status.
The eNodeB periodically checks whether multiple UEs in a cell need to be
scheduled in the downlink. If yes, the eNodeB allows the cell to enter the
frequency selective state. Smart frequency selective scheduling can be
performed on target UEs only in a cell in the frequency selective state.
-  Frequency selective scheduling
This procedure is the same as that in basic frequency selective scheduling.
Two-Dimensional Frequency Selective Scheduling
Two-dimensional frequency selective scheduling is specified by the
2D_FSS_SWITCH option of the CellAlgoExtSwitch.
DlSchEnhSwitch parameter.
The FreqSelSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter and the
SMART_FSS_SWITCH option of the CellAlgoSwitch.DlSchExtSwitch parameter
must be selected before two-dimensional frequency selective scheduling is
enabled. The main procedures include target UE selection, cell frequency selective
status determination, and frequency selective scheduling.
-  Target UE selection
Similar to smart frequency selective scheduling, two-dimensional frequency
selective scheduling uses aperiodic CQI reporting, Doppler measurement, and
large-packet UE identification results as inputs for target UE selection.
–   Aperiodic CQI reporting
Two-dimensional frequency selective scheduling processes aperiodic CQI
reporting in the same way as smart frequency selective scheduling.
–   Doppler measurement
Two-dimensional frequency selective scheduling processes Doppler
measurements in the same way as smart frequency selective scheduling.
–   Large-packet UE identification
In two-dimensional frequency selective scheduling,
CellDlschAlgo.
Dl2DFssUserDataVolThld is used to specify the data
volume threshold for identifying large-packet UEs. If the downlink data
volume of a UE is greater than or equal to the value of the
CellDlschAlgo.
Dl2DFssUserDataVolThld parameter, the UE is considered
as a large-packet UE. Two-dimensional frequency selective scheduling is
performed only for large-packet UEs.
-  Cell frequency selective status determination
Doppler measurement processing in two-dimensional frequency selective
scheduling is the same as that in smart frequency selective scheduling.
-  Frequency selective scheduling
During downlink RB allocation in two-dimensional frequency selective
scheduling, the eNodeB reorders RBGs based on the scheduling priorities of
subbands to determine a proper RBG scheduling sequence. In addition,
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.89

frequency selective scheduling precedence over retransmission is supported to
fully utilize the frequency selective gains of channels. It is recommended that
the CellDlschAlgo.RbgAllocStrategy parameter be set to ROUND_UP and
the SmallPktMcsSelectAlgoSw option of the CellAlgoSwitch.DlSchSwitch
parameter be selected to enable the eNodeB to allocate downlink resources
to UEs at the RBG level. After the preceding configurations are complete, the
downlink IBLER and MCS index decrease.
## 4.5.1.2 Network Analysis
## 4.5.1.2.1 Benefits
Functions in downlink frequency selective scheduling are recommended for a cell
that meets all of the following conditions:
●  Urban cell with Cell.
HighSpeedFlag set to LOW_SPEED
●Downlink Resource Block Utilizing Rate > 30%
●L.Traffic.ActiveUser.DL.Avg > 2
●  (L.Thrp.Time.DL – L.Thrp.Time.DL.RmvLastTTI)/L.Thrp.Time.DL > 20%
## NO TE
Functions in downlink frequency selective scheduling are not recommended in any of the
following scenarios:
●  The buffered traffic volumes of multiple UEs are constantly large, for example, in a
multi-UE packet injection test.
●  A drive test or downlink peak rate demonstration is being performed.
The gains are as follows:
●  Functions in downlink frequency selective scheduling leverage the difference
in channel quality between subbands and allocate UEs the resources in their
respective optimal subbands, increasing the downlink MCS index and User
## Downlink Average Throughput.
●  Using a short frequency selective scheduling evaluation period and enabling
fast Doppler measurement reporting accelerate triggering frequency selective
scheduling for low-speed UEs. This further increases User Downlink Average
Throughput for these UEs.
## 4.5.1.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
Functions in downlink frequency selective scheduling have the following impacts:
●  The CPU usage increases.
●  The number of aperiodic CQI reporting times increases, which may cause Cell
Uplink Average Throughput and average uplink user-perceived throughput
to decrease.
●  The number of UEs concurrently scheduled in the downlink increases, which
may cause the average number of PDCCH symbols to increase.
The average uplink user-perceived throughput and average number of PDCCH
symbols are defined as follows:
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.90

●  Average uplink user-perceived throughput = L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/
L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced
●  Average number of PDCCH symbols = (1 x L.ChMeas.PDCCH.SymNum.1 + 2
x L.ChMeas.PDCCH.SymNum.2 + 3 x L.ChMeas.PDCCH.SymNum.3 + 4 x
L.ChMeas.PDCCH.SymNum.4)/(L.ChMeas.PDCCH.SymNum.1 +
L.ChMeas.PDCCH.SymNum.2 + L.ChMeas.PDCCH.SymNum.3 +
L.ChMeas.PDCCH.SymNum.4)
If basic frequency selective scheduling is in effect, enabling smart frequency
selective scheduling possibly decreases the value of the L.ChMeas.PRB.DL.FSS.Avg
counter. In addition, the values of related uplink counters, such as the number of
aperiodic CQI reports from UEs involved in frequency selective scheduling, will
change. For more information about aperiodic CQI reporting, see
## Channel State
Management. The average number of PDCCH symbols may decrease, and the CPU
usage may further increase.
When downlink frequency selective scheduling and its subfunctions take effect,
aperiodic CQI reporting increases, which may increase the uplink and downlink
packet delay of voice services.
If aperiodic CQI reporting has been enabled on the live network and the reported
aperiodic CQI information is adequate for the eNodeB to use frequency selective
scheduling on UEs, the number of aperiodic CQI reporting times will not change
significantly.
In FDD, after frequency selective scheduling takes effect, you are advised to set
the CellPdcchAlgo.
CfiAdjCceThld parameter to an appropriate value (as
recommended in parameter reference) instead of INF to reduce an unnecessary
increase in the number of PDCCH symbols. You are also advised to select the
PdcchRobustnessEnhSw option of the CellPdcchAlgo.
PdcchAdjAlgoSwitch
parameter.
In FDD, if smart frequency selective scheduling is in effect, enabling two-
dimensional frequency selective scheduling possibly increases the value of the
L.ChMeas.PRB.DL.FSS.Avg counter. In addition, the values of related uplink
counters, such as the number of aperiodic CQI reports from UEs involved in
frequency selective scheduling, will change. The average number of PDCCH
symbols may increase, and the CPU usage may further increase.

## Boards
All boards support basic frequency selective scheduling, but the LBBPc does not
support smart frequency selective scheduling.
In FDD, the LBBPc and LBBPd do not support two-dimensional frequency selective
scheduling.
## NO TE
●  If smart frequency selective scheduling or two-dimensional frequency selective
scheduling is enabled for the LBBPc, only basic frequency selective scheduling takes
effect.
●  If two-dimensional frequency selective scheduling is enabled for the LBBPd, only smart
frequency selective scheduling takes effect.

4.5.1.4.2 Using MML Commands
Before using MML commands, confirm the related parameter modification
precautions by referring to the fields including "Service Interrupted After
Modification" and "Caution" for the corresponding parameters in the parameter
reference.
## Activation Command Examples
Before using MML commands, refer to 4.5.1.2.2 Impacts and 4.5.1.3.2 Software
and complete the parameter configurations for related functions based on the
impact and mutually exclusive relationships between the functions, as well as the
actual network scenario.
//Enabling basic frequency selective scheduling
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, DlSchSwitch=FreqSelSwitch-1;
//Enabling smart frequency selective scheduling
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, DlSchExtSwitch=SMART_FSS_SWITCH-1;
//Enabling two-dimensional frequency selective scheduling
MOD CELLALGOEXTSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, DlSchEnhSwitch=2D_FSS_SWITCH-1;
//Enabling the intra-RBG MCS index reduction switch in two-dimensional frequency selective scheduling
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, DlSchSwitch=SmallPktMcsSelectAlgoSw-1;
//Setting RBG Resource Allocation Strategy to ROUND_UP in two-dimensional frequency selective
scheduling
MOD CELLDLSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, RbgAllocStrategy=ROUND_UP;
## Optimization Command Examples
//Enabling the function of ignoring Doppler measurements in downlink frequency selective scheduling
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, DlSchSwitch=FreqSelJudgeIgnorDopplerSwitch-1;
//Configuring the frequency selective scheduling evaluation period
MOD CELLDLSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, FreqSelJudgePeriod=1;
//Enabling fast Doppler measurement reporting and setting the Doppler measurement level
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, DopMeasLevel=CLASS_1, DopAlgoSwitch=FastReportDopSwitch-1;
//Configuring the switch for dynamic adjustment of the number of PDCCH OFDM symbols
MOD CELLPDCCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, PdcchSymNumSwitch=ECFIADAPTIONON;
//Turning on the aperiodic CQI compatibility switch
MOD ENODEBALGOSWITCH: CompatibilityCtrlSwitch=ApCqiAndAckAbnCtrlSwitch-1;
//Turning on the aperiodic CQI reporting optimization switch
MOD CELLALGOEXTSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, DlSchEnhSwitch=APERIODIC_CQI_RPT_OPT_SW-1;
//Configuring the data volume threshold of UEs in two-dimensional frequency selective scheduling
MOD CELLDLSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, Dl2DFssUserDataVolThld=50;
//Configuring the downlink UE data volume threshold
MOD CELLDLSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, DlUserDataThld=500;
## Deactivation Command Examples
The following provides only deactivation command examples. You can determine
whether to restore the settings of other parameters based on actual network
conditions.
//Disabling two-dimensional frequency selective scheduling
MOD CELLALGOEXTSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, DlSchEnhSwitch=2D_FSS_SWITCH-0;
//Disabling smart frequency selective scheduling
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, DlSchExtSwitch=SMART_FSS_SWITCH-0;
//Disabling frequency selective scheduling
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, DlSchSwitch=FreqSelSwitch-0;
4.5.1.4.3 Using the MAE-Deployment
For detailed operations, see Feature Configuration Using the MAE-Deployment.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.98

## 4.5.1.4.4 Activation Verification
## Using Counters
After downlink frequency selective scheduling is enabled, check the value of
L.ChMeas.PRB.DL.FSS.Avg. If the value of this counter is not 0, downlink
frequency selective scheduling has taken effect.
## Using Monitoring Tasks
Use the MAE-Access to verify whether downlink frequency selective scheduling
has taken effect. Perform the following steps:
-  Enable a UE to access a cell.
-  On the MAE-Access, start a cell RB usage monitoring task.
-  Check the number of RBs used in downlink frequency selective scheduling. If
the value of Downlink Freq Select Used RB Num is not zero, downlink
frequency selective scheduling has taken effect.
## 4.5.1.4.5 Network Monitoring
After downlink frequency selective scheduling is enabled, the downlink throughput
per RB is expected to increase.
Calculate the downlink throughput per RB using the following formula: Downlink
throughput per RB = L.Thrp.bits.DL/L.ChMeas.PRB.DL.Used.Avg/Counter_period.
In this formula, Counter_period is the measurement period in ms.
Monitor the downlink MCS index through the counters L.ChMeas.PDSCH.MCS.0
to L.ChMeas.PDSCH.MCS.28.
Downlink IBLER = (L.Traffic.DL.SCH.QPSK.ErrTB.Ibler +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.16QAM.ErrTB.Ibler + L.Traffic.DL.SCH.64QAM.ErrTB.Ibler +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.256QAM.ErrTB.Ibler)/(L.Traffic.DL.SCH.QPSK.TB +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.16QAM.TB + L.Traffic.DL.SCH.64QAM.TB +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.256QAM.TB)
Downlink packet delay of voice services = L.Traffic.DL.PktDelay.Time.QCI.1/
L.Traffic.DL.PktDelay.Num.QCI.1
An increase in the uplink packet delay of voice services may cause uplink voice
quality to deteriorate. Specifically, there is a tendency towards low quality as
indicated by the value distribution ranges of the following voice quality indicator
(VQI) counters:
●L.Voice.VQI.UL.Excellent.Times
●L.Voice.VQI.UL.Good.Times
●L.Voice.VQI.UL.Accept.Times
●L.Voice.VQI.UL.Poor.Times
●L.Voice.VQI.UL.Bad.Times
4.5.2 Adaptive Power Allocation for Cell-Edge UEs (FDD)
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.99

## 4.5.2.1 Principles
Adaptive power allocation for cell-edge UEs aims to increase the downlink
throughput of cell-edge UEs, for which the indexes of selected MCSs are less than
- If channels are not additive white Gaussian noise (AWGN) channels and radio
resources are insufficient, this function reallocates RBs and reselects an MCS for a
cell-edge UE after RB allocation for initial transmission. This increases the TBS for
the UE without changing its total power. This function is controlled by the
AdptCellEdgePwrAllocSw option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
CellDlCoverEnhanceSwitch parameter.
## 4.5.2.2 Network Analysis
## 4.5.2.2.1 Benefits
In light-load wide-coverage scenarios (such as rural areas, deserts, or oceans),
adaptive power allocation for cell-edge UEs increases the average downlink
throughput of cell-edge UEs allocated limited radio resources.

4.5.2.4.3 Using the MAE-Deployment
For detailed operations, see Feature Configuration Using the MAE-Deployment.
## 4.5.2.4.4 Activation Verification
-  Before activating adaptive power allocation for cell-edge UEs, calculate the
percentage of PRBs scheduled for PDSCH transmission using rank 1 and MCSs
of indexes 0 to 6. The formula for calculating this percentage is as follows:
Percentage of PRBs scheduled for PDSCH transmission using rank 1 and MCSs
of indexes 0 to 6 = Sum of the values of the counters from
L.ChMeas.PRB.DL.RANK1.MCS.0 to L.ChMeas.PRB.DL.RANK1.MCS.6/Sum of
the values of the counters from L.ChMeas.PRB.DL.RANK1.MCS.0 to
L.ChMeas.PRB.DL.RANK1.MCS.28
-  Activate adaptive power allocation for cell-edge UEs, and then calculate this
PRB percentage again. If this percentage decreases, this function has taken
effect.
## 4.5.2.4.5 Network Monitoring
Use the following formula to obtain the average downlink throughput of cell-edge
UEs: (L.Thrp.bits.DL.BorderUE – L.Thrp.bits.DL.LastTTI.BorderUE)/
L.Thrp.Time.DL.RmvLastTTI.BorderUE. After this function is enabled, the average
downlink throughput of cell-edge UEs increases.
## NO TE
To use the preceding counters, select the BasedA3EdgeUserSwitch option of the
CellCounterParaGroup.CellCounterAlgoSwitch parameter.
4.5.3 Preferential Allocation of High-Frequency Resources
## (FDD)
## 4.5.3.1 Principles
During downlink scheduling, the eNodeB starts RB allocation from the low end of
the cell's frequency range in most cases. If the
CellIntrfCtrlParam.
DlRbAllocationDirection parameter is set to HIGH_TO_LOW,
preferential allocation of high-frequency resources is enabled. With this function,
the eNodeB starts RB allocation from the high end of the cell's frequency range to
reduce downlink interference on the low end of the frequency range.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.104

## NO TE
If resource allocation type 1 is used for downlink scheduling, there is an extremely low
probability that RB allocation does not start from the high end to the low end of the
frequency range. However, this does not affect the effect of reducing interference on the
low end of the frequency range.
## 4.5.3.2 Network Analysis
## 4.5.3.2.1 Benefits
Preferential allocation of high-frequency resources reduces downlink interference
on the low end of the cell's frequency range by allowing RB allocation for
downlink scheduling to start from the high end of the frequency range.

4.5.4 Restricted Allocation of Downlink RBs (FDD)
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.107

## 4.5.4.1 Principles
Restricted allocation of downlink RBs restricts the total number of RBs that can be
allocated within each TTI during downlink scheduling to reduce downlink co-
channel interference on surrounding cells. The parameters involved are as follows:
●CellIntrfCtrlParam.LowIntrfStateRbUsageThld: This parameter is used to
specify the upper limit on the percentage of RBs allocated in each TTI when
the low-interference policy is used. If a cell is in the low-interference state, the
downlink RB usage per TTI cannot exceed this limit.
●CellIntrfCtrlParam.
LowIntrfStateDuration: This parameter is used to specify
the low-interference policy duration.
●CellIntrfCtrlParam.
HighIntrfStateRbUsageThld: This parameter is used to
specify the upper limit on the percentage of RBs allocated in each TTI when
the high-interference policy is used. If a cell is in the high-interference state,
the downlink RB usage per TTI cannot exceed this limit.
●CellIntrfCtrlParam.
HighIntrfStateDuration: This parameter is used to specify
the high-interference policy duration.
Restricted allocation of downlink RBs is enabled or disabled depending on the
following parameter settings:
●  This function is enabled if either CellIntrfCtrlParam.
LowIntrfStateDuration
or CellIntrfCtrlParam.HighIntrfStateDuration is set to a value other than 0.
The eNodeB first uses the parameter settings for the high-interference policy
and then uses those for the low-interference policy to restrict the total
number of RBs that can be allocated within each TTI and also restrict the
duration in which this number takes effect. Subsequently, the eNodeB
alternates between the two policies in the same order.
●  This function is disabled if both the
CellIntrfCtrlParam.
LowIntrfStateDuration and
CellIntrfCtrlParam.
HighIntrfStateDuration parameters are set to 0.
## NO TE
●  The maximum number of actually allocated RBs may be different from the parameter
value on the GUI. This is because RBG-based resource allocation is used in the downlink
and the number of allocated RBs cannot be precisely controlled.
●  This function takes effect only for initial transmissions of data services, but does not
restrict the number of RBs used for control-plane scheduling or for retransmissions of
data services. This also leads to the difference between the maximum number of
actually allocated RBs and the number specified by the parameter on the GUI.
## 4.5.4.2 Network Analysis
## 4.5.4.2.1 Benefits
Restricted allocation of downlink RBs reduces the downlink co-channel
interference on surrounding cells by restricting the total number of downlink RBs
that can be allocated in the local cell.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.108

## 4.5.4.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
Due to restriction on the total number of downlink RBs that can be allocated, the
values of service-related performance indicators such as User Downlink Average
Throughput and Cell Downlink Average Throughput are adversely affected.

4.6 MCS Selection During Downlink Scheduling
4.6.1 Downlink AMC
## 4.6.1.1 Principles
Adaptive modulation and coding (AMC) is a basic procedure used during
scheduling. In this procedure, the eNodeB selects appropriate MCSs for UEs based
on current channel quality.

4.6.2 Optimization of MCS Selection for Handover
## 4.6.2.1 Principles
A UE experiences relatively poor channel quality during and immediately after a
handover. To ensure stable data transmissions in this situation, the eNodeB uses
different MCS selection policies before and after the timer specified by the
CellDlschAlgo.
HoStaticMcsTimer parameter expires in the target cell.
●  Before the timer expires, the eNodeB selects a fixed MCS for the UE.
●  After the timer expires, the eNodeB selects an MCS for the UE dynamically.
## 4.6.2.2 Network Analysis
## 4.6.2.2.1 Benefits
Setting the timer in the target cell ensures stable downlink data transmission
during the handover.

4.6.3 MCS Selection with Prioritized RBs
## 4.6.3.1 Principles
MCS selection with prioritized RBs reduces the MCS index and increases the
number of RBs. When the air interface resources are sufficient, this function
reduces retransmissions without changing the TBS, and therefore reduces the
scheduling delay of small-traffic services, which increases UE data rates.
The eNodeB measures the ratio of TTIs during which the MCS index is reduced for
scheduling to the total downlink subframes in each measurement period. If the
ratio is less than the threshold specified by the
CellDlschAlgo.
RBPriMcsSelectRatioThd parameter for the measurement period,
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.115

MCS selection with prioritized RBs will be enabled in the next measurement
period.
●  If this parameter is set to 0, MCS selection with prioritized RBs is disabled.
●  If this parameter is set to 100, MCS selection with prioritized RBs is always
enabled.
MCS selection with prioritized RBs applies to single-UE and multi-UE scenarios.
●  Single-UE scenarios
–   There is a single online UE with limited capabilities.
–   There is only one service to be scheduled and the percentage of TTIs
where surplus RBs are expected to be available is less than the value of
the CellDlschAlgo.
RBPriMcsSelectRatioThd parameter in a
measurement period.
●  Multi-UE scenarios
MCS selection with prioritized RBs can be used for the last UE to be scheduled
within the current TTI, which can be either of the following:
–   The last UE that is to be scheduled in the TTI and has limited capabilities
–   The last UE to be scheduled in the current TTI when the percentage of
TTIs where surplus RBs are expected to be available is less than the
CellDlschAlgo.
RBPriMcsSelectRatioThd parameter value in a
measurement period
The CellDlschAlgo.
RBPriMcsSelectStrategy parameter determines the policy for
MCS selection with prioritized RBs.
●  If this parameter is set to SINGLEUSER_STRATEGY, MCS selection with
prioritized RBs is performed only in single-UE scenarios.
●  If this parameter is set to MULTIUSER_STRATEGY, MCS selection with
prioritized RBs can be performed in either single- or multi-UE scenarios.
●  If this parameter is set to MULTIUSR_LOWMCSLIMIT_STRATEGY, enhanced
MCS selection with prioritized RBs can be performed in either single-UE
scenarios or multi-UE scenarios. Enhanced MCS selection with prioritized RBs
allows the MCS index to be decreased by a maximum of 3.
In addition, further optimization of MCS selection with prioritized RBs is controlled
using the CellDlschAlgo.
RbPriMcsSelectMaxDecTbsIdx parameter.
●  If the CellDlschAlgo.RbPriMcsSelectMaxDecTbsIdx parameter is set to 255,
this optimization does not take effect.
●  If the CellDlschAlgo.
RbPriMcsSelectMaxDecTbsIdx parameter is set to a
value other than 255, this optimization takes effect. The value of this
parameter indicates the maximum decrease in the TBS index when the single-
UE policy or multi-UE policy is used.
–   When CellAlgoSwitch.
InterfRandSwitch is set to ENB_BASED, the
eNodeB restricts the range of RBs for which the index can be reduced for
the to-be-scheduled UEs.
–   When CellAlgoSwitch.
InterfRandSwitch is set to OFF, the eNodeB does
not restrict the range of RBs for which the index can be reduced for the
to-be-scheduled UEs.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.116

## 4.6.3.2 Network Analysis
## 4.6.3.2.1 Benefits
MCS selection with prioritized RBs reduces the number of retransmissions and the
scheduling delay of small-traffic services, which increases User Downlink Average
## Throughput.
It is recommended that optimized MCS selection with prioritized RBs be enabled
when both of the following conditions are met:
●  The downlink RB usage is less than 30%.
●  The proportion of last TTIs is greater than 20%.
●  The CellDlschAlgo.
RBPriMcsSelectRatioThd parameter is set to 10%.
In this case, optimized MCS selection with prioritized RBs increases User
Downlink Average Throughput by 2–5%. If the preceding conditions are not met,
enabling this optimization does not bring negative gains.
Proportion of last TTIs = (L.Thrp.Time.DL – L.Thrp.Time.DL.RmvLastTTI)/
L.Thrp.Time.DL
## 4.6.3.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
If the load of a cell is light, for example the downlink RB usage is less than 20%,
MCS selection with prioritized RBs reduces air interface delay for a larger number
of UEs with small-traffic services when the setting of the
CellDlschAlgo.
RBPriMcsSelectStrategy parameter is changed from
SINGLEUSER_STRATEGY to MULTIUSR_LOWMCSLIMIT_STRATEGY. However, the
IBLER increases for UEs with poor channel quality, for example in a suburb, and
the scheduling delay and downlink packet loss rate also increase for these UEs,
especially for UEs running voice services. This is due to the fact that the MCS index
can be reduced by 3 at most.

## Activation Command Examples
Before using MML commands, refer to 4.6.3.2.2 Impacts and complete the
parameter configurations for related functions based on the impact relationships
between the functions, as well as the actual network scenario.
//Setting the RB Priority MCS Select Strategy and RB Priority MCS Select Ratio Threshold parameters
MOD CELLDLSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, RBPriMcsSelectRatioThd=10,
RBPriMcsSelectStrategy=MULTIUSR_LOWMCSLIMIT_STRATEGY;
## Optimization Command Examples
When optimized MCS selection with prioritized RBs is required, perform the
following configurations:
//Reconfiguring the RB Priority MCS Select Strategy and RB Priority MCS Select Ratio Threshold parameters
MOD CELLDLSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, RBPriMcsSelectRatioThd=100,
RBPriMcsSelectStrategy=MULTIUSER_STRATEGY;
//Two scenarios of setting the maximum decrease in the TBS index for MCS selection with prioritized RBs
## ●  Scenario 1:
//Setting the maximum decrease in the TBS index to 4 for MCS selection with prioritized RBs when
Interference Randomize Algorithm Switch is set to ENB_BASED
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, InterfRandSwitch=ENB_BASED;
MOD CELLDLSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, RbPriMcsSelectMaxDecTbsIdx=4;
## ●  Scenario 2:
//Setting the maximum decrease in the TBS index to 6 for MCS selection with prioritized RBs when
Interference Randomize Algorithm Switch is set to OFF
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, InterfRandSwitch=OFF;
MOD CELLDLSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, RbPriMcsSelectMaxDecTbsIdx=6;
## Deactivation Command Examples
The following provides only deactivation command examples. You can determine
whether to restore the settings of other parameters based on actual network
conditions.
//Disabling MCS selection with prioritized RBs
MOD CELLDLSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, RBPriMcsSelectRatioThd=0,
RBPriMcsSelectStrategy=MULTIUSR_LOWMCSLIMIT_STRATEGY;
4.6.3.4.3 Using the MAE-Deployment
For detailed operations, see Feature Configuration Using the MAE-Deployment.
## 4.6.3.4.4 Activation Verification
Check the downlink IBLER and downlink average MCS index before and after
enabling MCS selection with prioritized RBs. If they decrease after this function is
enabled, this function has taken effect.
## 4.6.3.4.5 Network Monitoring
●  Downlink IBLER
Downlink IBLER = (L.Traffic.DL.SCH.QPSK.ErrTB.Ibler +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.16QAM.ErrTB.Ibler + L.Traffic.DL.SCH.64QAM.ErrTB.Ibler +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.256QAM.ErrTB.Ibler)/(L.Traffic.DL.SCH.QPSK.TB +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.16QAM.TB + L.Traffic.DL.SCH.64QAM.TB +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.256QAM.TB)
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.122

●  Downlink average MCS index
Downlink average MCS index = (L.ChMeas.PDSCH.MCS.0 x 0 +
L.ChMeas.PDSCH.MCS.1 x 1 + ... + L.ChMeas.PDSCH.MCS.28 x 28)/Sum of
the values of L.ChMeas.PDSCH.MCS.0 to L.ChMeas.PDSCH.MCS.28
4.6.4 MCS Selection for Transmission of Small Amounts of
## Data
## 4.6.4.1 Principles
If the amount of downlink data to be initially transmitted is small, the TBS
selected for the initial transmission may be greater than the amount of data to be
transmitted. In this situation, the SmallPktMcsSelectAlgoSw option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter can be selected to enable MCS selection
for transmission of small amounts of data. When this option is selected, the
eNodeB selects an MCS with a smaller index while maintaining the number of
allocated RBs. This reduces transmissions of invalid data and the number of
retransmissions and increases average downlink UE throughput.
After the SmallPktMcsSelectAlgoSw option is selected, the eNodeB determines
whether to adjust the CQI of a UE based on the HARQ feedback for the transport
block for which the MCS index has been decreased.
●  If the HARQ feedback is NACK, the eNodeB adjusts the CQI.
●  In FDD, if the HARQ feedback is ACK:
–   If the SMALL_PKT_MCS_CQI_ADJ_SW option of the
CellAlgoExtSwitch.
DlSchEnhSwitch parameter is selected, the eNodeB
does not adjust the CQI.
–   In other cases, the eNodeB still adjusts the CQI.
For details about CQI adjustment, see
## Channel State Management.
## 4.6.4.2 Network Analysis
## 4.6.4.2.1 Benefits
MCS selection for transmission of small amounts of data reduces the number of
retransmissions and the scheduling delay of small-traffic services, which increases
## User Downlink Average Throughput.
## 4.6.4.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
In FDD, if the SMALL_PKT_MCS_CQI_ADJ_SW option of the
CellAlgoExtSwitch.
DlSchEnhSwitch parameter is selected, the cell overall MCS
index and downlink IBLER may decrease when the small-packet and large-packet
hybrid service model is used.
## Function Impacts
## None

## 4.6.4.4.5 Network Monitoring
Use the counters L.ChMeas.PDSCH.MCS.0 to L.ChMeas.PDSCH.MCS.28 to
monitor downlink MCS index distribution. After this function is enabled, downlink
MCS index distribution indicates a general index change towards lower index
ranges.
4.6.5 MCS Selection Enhancement for Signaling
## 4.6.5.1 Principles
If a fixed low-order MCS is used for downlink signaling transmission to ensure the
transmission reliability, RB resources are wasted when channel quality is favorable
for UEs. In the event of concurrent UE data and signaling transmission, the
UeSigMcsEnhanceSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter
can be selected to adapt the MCS to the signal quality of UEs, which optimizes the
MCS distribution and improves the downlink spectral efficiency.
## 4.6.5.2 Network Analysis
## 4.6.5.2.1 Benefits
MCS selection enhancement for signaling helps increase the downlink spectral
efficiency.

4.6.6 Scheduling Optimization in Case of Pa Value
## Inconsistency
## 4.6.6.1 Principles
Maximum likelihood detection (MLD) receivers require that the Pa values of the
base station and UE be consistent; otherwise, performance may deteriorate in
scenarios with high channel correlation and SINR. Scheduling optimization in case
of Pa value inconsistency can be enabled by selecting the
PA_MISMATCH_SCH_OPT_SWITCH option of the
CellAlgoExtSwitch.
DlSchEnhSwitch parameter. When enabled, this function
ensures data transmission reliability. It is recommended that the
PaReconfigOptSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
DlSchSwitch parameter also
be selected when this function is enabled.
## 4.6.6.2 Network Analysis
## 4.6.6.2.1 Benefits
Scheduling optimization in case of Pa value inconsistency ensures the reliability of
the Pa reconfiguration signaling sent by the eNodeB to UEs and the transmission
reliability of initially transmitted data in case of Pa value inconsistency.
eRAN

## 4.6.6.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
Scheduling optimization in case of Pa value inconsistency decreases the downlink
IBLER but may slightly decrease Cell Downlink Average Throughput.

4.6.7 Downlink MCS Selection Optimization (FDD)
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.131

## 4.6.7.1 Principles
MCS Selection Optimization for Initial Transmissions
With MCS selection optimization for initial transmissions, the IBLER measured in
the previous period is compared with the target IBLER and the ACK for MCS index
reduction in the current period is adaptively used for CQI adjustment based on the
comparison result. This function is controlled by the
CellCqiAdjAlgo.
IblerTargetOffset parameter. If this parameter is set to 255, this
function does not take effect. If this parameter is set to a value other than 255,
this function takes effect and this parameter specifies the offset of the measured
IBLER relative to the target IBLER.
●  When the measured IBLER in the previous period is greater than the target
IBLER plus the offset specified by CellCqiAdjAlgo.
IblerTargetOffset, the UE
BLER is relatively high and the ACK for MCS index reduction in the current
period is not used for CQI adjustment.
●  When the measured IBLER in the previous period is less than the target IBLER
minus the offset specified by CellCqiAdjAlgo.
IblerTargetOffset, the UE BLER
is relatively low and the ACK for MCS index reduction in the current period is
used for CQI adjustment.
●  When the measured IBLER in the previous period ranges from the result of
the target IBLER minus the offset specified by
CellCqiAdjAlgo.
IblerTargetOffset to the result of the target IBLER plus this
offset, the ACK for MCS index reduction in the current period is used for CQI
adjustment based on a certain weighting proportion.
MCS Selection Optimization for Retransmissions
MCS selection optimization for retransmissions is controlled by the
RETRANS_OPT_ENH_SW option of the CellAlgoExtSwitch.
DlSchEnhSwitch
parameter. If this option is selected, the eNodeB works as follows:
●  Allocates more RBs for initial transmission when there are remaining RBs in a
cell, a UE has both retransmission and initial transmission requests to be
scheduled, and the number of RBs required for initial transmission is greater
than that for retransmission.
●  Increases the TBS indexes used for the first and second downlink HARQ
retransmissions to spare RBs for initial transmission scheduling if no
remaining RB resources are expected to be available in a cell.
The amount of the increase is determined by the
CellDlschAlgo.
DlFirstHarqTxTbsIncNum parameter.
## 4.6.7.2 Network Analysis
## 4.6.7.2.1 Benefits
MCS selection optimization for initial transmissions accelerates the IBLER
convergence. MCS selection optimization for retransmissions increases the
downlink RB usage.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description4 Downlink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.132

MCS selection optimization for initial transmissions and retransmissions increases
User Downlink Average Throughput by 1% to 5% when any of the following
conditions is met:
●  The CellDlschAlgo.RbgAllocStrategy parameter is set to ROUND_UP and the
SmallPktMcsSelectAlgoSw option of the CellAlgoSwitch.DlSchSwitch
parameter is selected.
●  The CellDlschAlgo.RBPriMcsSelectRatioThd parameter is set to 100.
If none of the preceding conditions is met, this function increases User Downlink
Average Throughput less significantly but does not bring negative impacts.
## 4.6.7.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
After downlink MCS selection optimization takes effect, the downlink RBLER may
increase.
## NO TE
Downlink RBLER = (L.Traffic.DL.SCH.QPSK.ErrTB.Rbler +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.16QAM.ErrTB.Rbler + L.Traffic.DL.SCH.64QAM.ErrTB.Rbler +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.256QAM.ErrTB.Rbler)/(L.Traffic.DL.SCH.QPSK.TB +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.16QAM.TB + L.Traffic.DL.SCH.64QAM.TB +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.256QAM.TB)

## 5 Uplink Scheduling
Unless otherwise specified, average uplink user-perceived throughput mentioned
in "Benefits" and "Impacts" sections in this document is defined as follows:
Average uplink user-perceived throughput = L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/
L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced
## 5.1 Uplink Scheduling Resource Acquisition
Before the scheduler starts uplink scheduling, it needs to obtain available uplink
resources, which mainly refer to PUSCH RB resources. This section describes
variations of available PUSCH RBs with scenarios as well as the variations caused
by manual blocking of PUSCH RBs.
5.1.1 Calculation of Available PUSCH RBs
## 5.1.1.1 Principles
In the uplink of an LTE system, the PUSCH, PUCCH, and PRACH share the entire
uplink bandwidth.
●  The PUCCH occupies RBs at two ends of a cell bandwidth, and the number of
RBs is dependent on the number of UEs in semi-persistent scheduling mode
and the amount of resources required to carry information on the PUCCH.
The information includes HARQ feedback to downlink dynamic scheduling,
CQIs indicating downlink channel quality, and SRIs. For details on the PUCCH,
see
## Physical Channel Resource Management.
●  The PRACH occupies six RBs in the uplink.
●  The number of PUSCH RBs allocated to a UE is
, where , ,
and
are not minus integers.
Available PUSCH RBs vary as follows:
●  Generally, the number of available PUSCH RBs is equal to the number of RBs
across the entire channel bandwidth minus the number of RBs occupied by
the PUCCH and PRACH.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.136

●  If the PrachRbReuseSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchSwitch
parameter is selected, the PUSCH can occupy PRACH RBs.
●  When the PuschUsePucchRbSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchSwitch parameter is selected:
–   If the cell is serving only one UE, the PUSCH can occupy PUCCH RBs.
–   If the cell is serving more than one UE, the PUSCH cannot occupy PUCCH
RBs.
## 5.1.1.2 Network Analysis
## 5.1.1.2.1 Benefits
A rise in the number of available PUSCH RBs increases uplink UE peak throughput.
## 5.1.1.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
When the PuschUsePucchRbSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchSwitch
parameter is selected, the PRB usage may decrease if the PUCCH RBs occupied by
the PUSCH are not used.

5.1.2 Manual Blocking of PUSCH RBs
## 5.1.2.1 Principles
If certain PUSCH RBs experience strong interference from a specific external source
or are unavailable because of spectrum planning, these RBs can be manually
blocked so that the eNodeB will not schedule them in the uplink.
The parameters in the CellRbReserve MO determine the RB blocking mode and
the positions and quantity of these RBs. This RB blocking function does not take
effect on control channels or common channels. Therefore, it is recommended that
the blocked PUSCH RBs not overlap the RBs occupied by the PUCCH and PRACH.

5.2 Optimization of Uplink HARQ Retransmissions
5.2.1 Retransmission Upon Detection of PUSCH DTX
## 5.2.1.1 Principles
Generally, the result of PUSCH data reception by the eNodeB is ACK or NACK.
Given that NACK is possibly caused by no data transmission from the UE side,
PUSCH DTX detection can be enabled on the eNodeB to detect such situation and
then the result of PUSCH data reception can be ACK, NACK, or DTX. In FDD, the
PUSCH DTX detection function is controlled by the PuschDtxSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter.
●  If the result of PUSCH data reception by the eNodeB is ACK, the data
transmission ends.
●  If the result of PUSCH data reception by the eNodeB is NACK, the UE is
instructed to perform data retransmission.
●  If the result of PUSCH data reception by the eNodeB is DTX, uplink SINRs are
not adjusted subsequently.
In FDD, the CellUlschAlgo.
PuschDtxSchStrategy parameter determines the
HARQ process scheduling policy for a data packet.
–   If this parameter is set to NEW_TX, the eNodeB stops data retransmission
for the HARQ process and delivers UE initial-transmission scheduling
information on the PDCCH.
–   If this parameter is set to ADAPTIVE_RETX, the eNodeB adopts adaptive
HARQ retransmission scheduling for the HARQ process to deliver the
same uplink scheduling information over the PDCCH as that in the initial
transmission, including the indicated MCS and number of RBs. If DRX has
been activated, adaptive HARQ retransmission scheduling is performed
only when the On Duration Timer specified by the
DrxParaGroup.
OnDurationTimer parameter is running.
–   If this parameter is set to EN_ADAPTIVE_RETX and DRX is not activated,
the eNodeB uses adaptive HARQ retransmission scheduling for the HARQ
process to deliver the same uplink scheduling information over the
PDCCH as that in the initial transmission, including the indicated MCS
and number of RBs. However, if DRX has been activated, adaptive HARQ
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.144

retransmission scheduling is performed only during the active time of the
## UE.
## 5.2.1.2 Network Analysis
## 5.2.1.2.1 Benefits
When PUSCH DTX detection is enabled, the uplink scheduler stops invalid
retransmissions and performs initial transmissions based on the PUSCH DTX
detection result, saving uplink resources and increasing Cell Uplink Average
Throughput and average uplink user-perceived throughput. (Average uplink user-
perceived throughput = L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/
L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced)

5.2.2 Uplink HARQ Retransmission Optimization
## 5.2.2.1 Principles
If consecutive CRC errors occur in a HARQ retransmission process, a reduced code
rate can be used for HARQ retransmissions when the cell serves less than 150
RRC_CONNECTED UEs and idle RBs are available. The TBS and MCS used for
HARQ retransmissions are the same as those used for the initial transmission.
Therefore, more RBs need to be allocated for HARQ retransmissions.
The UlLast2RetransSchOptSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch
parameter specifies whether uplink HARQ retransmissions use a reduced code
rate. When this option is selected, a reduced code rate is used for the last two
uplink HARQ retransmissions and more RBs are allocated to the two
retransmissions.
In adaptive uplink HARQ retransmissions for voice services, the MCS selection
policy is specified by the UlVoipRblerControlSwitch option of the
CellUlschAlgo.
UlEnhencedVoipSchSw parameter.
●  If this option is selected, the scheduler selects lower-order MCSs for voice
services than for data services.
●  If this option is deselected, the scheduler uses the same policy to select the
MCSs for voice and data services.
If the UlLast2RetransSchOptSwitch and UlVoipRblerControlSwitch options are
both selected, the eNodeB increases the number of RBs and reduces the code rate
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.147

also for voice services in the last two HARQ retransmissions. The eNodeB increases
the number of RBs for the retransmissions even if the retransmissions conflict with
measurement gaps.
## 5.2.2.2 Network Analysis
## 5.2.2.2.1 Benefits
Optimization of uplink HARQ retransmissions reduces the uplink RBLER and
## Service Drop Rate.
## 5.2.2.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
Optimization of uplink HARQ retransmissions increases the number of uplink RBs
used for retransmissions and therefore may slightly reduce Cell Uplink Average
## Throughput.

5.3 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
For details on the basic policies used in UE selection for uplink scheduling, see
3.2.2.3 Basic Uplink Scheduling Policies. The scheduler acts differently in UE
selection for some special scenarios. The UE selection functions used in special
scenarios include the following:
●  Uplink scheduling termination for abnormal UEs
●  SRI detection optimization
●  PDCCH and PUSCH resource allocation optimization
●  Proactive scheduling for uplink signaling
●  Optimization on false SR detection in DRX mode
## ●  Preallocation
●  Outer-loop DSCP-based preallocation
●  Scheduling restriction on abnormal UEs
●  Preallocation for NSA DC UEs (FDD)
●  Uplink short-interval SPS (FDD)
5.3.1 Uplink Scheduling Termination for Abnormal UEs
## 5.3.1.1 Principles
Termination of the uplink scheduling of UEs in extremely weak coverage areas or
with radio link failures prevents these UEs from overusing system resources. When
the AbnUeSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
CellSchStrategySwitch
parameter is selected, the uplink scheduler of the eNodeB determines whether the
UE link is abnormal based on uplink demodulation performance. Specifically, if the
number of successive times that the uplink CRC for a UE fails exceeds the value of
the CellUlschAlgo.
UlSchAbnUeThd parameter, the uplink scheduler determines
that demodulation performance is poor and the UE link is abnormal. In this
situation, the eNodeB clears the maintained buffer size settings of the UE to stop
uplink scheduling for the UE.
The eNodeB attempts to schedule a UE if it receives two consecutive SRs from the
UE. If the CRC for the UE succeeds, the uplink scheduler clears the abnormal state
of the UE link and the UE can be scheduled normally. If the CRC fails, the UE link
continues to be in the abnormal state.
## 5.3.1.2 Network Analysis
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.150

## 5.3.1.2.1 Benefits
Uplink scheduling termination for abnormal UEs (those with links in the abnormal
state) saves system resources, increasing the Cell Uplink Average Throughput
and decreasing the uplink RBLER in case of resource insufficiency.
## 5.3.1.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
Uplink scheduling termination for abnormal UEs reduces the scheduling
opportunities and increases the scheduling delay for UEs under weak coverage.
This causes the average uplink UE throughput to decrease and the service drop
rate to increase for these UEs.

5.3.2 SRI Detection Optimization
## 5.3.2.1 Principles
When no data is transmitted in the uplink, there is a low probability of false SRI
detection that causes the eNodeB to maintain incorrect information about UE DRX
state. In this case, the CellUlschAlgo.
SriFalseDetThdSwitch parameter can be set
to ON to increase the SRI false detection threshold for UEs in DRX state, reducing
the probability of false SRI detection.
Inaccurate measurements of interference and noise on the PUCCH possibly cause
the probability of SRI false detection to rise. The SriDetectEnhanceSW option of
the CellPucchAlgo.
SriAlgoSwitch parameter can be selected to improve the
accuracy of such measurements and therefore reduce the probability of SRI false
detection.
## 5.3.2.2 Network Analysis
## 5.3.2.2.1 Benefits
SRI detection optimization reduces the probability of false SRI detection and
therefore decreases the uplink RBLER.
Uplink RBLER = (L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.ErrTB.Rbler +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.ErrTB.Rbler + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.ErrTB.Rbler +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.ErrTB.Rbler)/(L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.TB + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.TB)
## 5.3.2.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
## None
## Function Impacts
## None

5.3.3 PDCCH and PUSCH Resource Allocation Optimization
## 5.3.3.1 Principles
The number of UEs scheduled per TTI in the uplink is limited in a cell if the cell
serves a large number of RRC_CONNECTED UEs, a high percentage of the UEs is
running small-packet services or located at the cell edge, and PDCCH resources
are insufficient.
In this situation, PDCCH and PUSCH resource allocation optimization can be used.
With this function, the scheduler randomly selects a UE engaged in large-packet
data transmission at the cell center and reserves PDCCH resources for the UE to
ensure that the selected UE can be scheduled, improving the uplink RB usage and
uplink traffic volume. Figure 5-1 illustrates the principles for PDCCH and PUSCH
resource allocation optimization.
Figure 5-1 Principles for PDCCH and PUSCH resource allocation optimization
This optimization involves the following parameters:
●CellUlschAlgo.DataThdInPdcchPuschBal. This parameter specifies the data
volume threshold for determining UEs with large-packet data transmission.
●CellUlschAlgo.UeNumThdInPdcchPuschBal. This parameter specifies the UE
number threshold for triggering this optimization in a cell.
Note the following when using this optimization:
●  In RAN sharing scenarios, this optimization is not recommended because
there is a probability that the operator-specific resource occupation ratio is
not strictly applied due to random selection of a UE with large-packet data
transmission.
●  It is good practice to select the SchedulerCtrlPowerSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter when using this optimization,
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.156

preventing UEs with large-packet data transmission from occupying excess
resources.
## 5.3.3.2 Network Analysis
## 5.3.3.2.1 Benefits
PDCCH and PUSCH resource allocation optimization increases the uplink RB usage
and therefore increases Cell Uplink Average Throughput.

5.3.3.4.3 Using the MAE-Deployment
For detailed operations, see Feature Configuration Using the MAE-Deployment.
## 5.3.3.4.4 Activation Verification
-  Check that the cell meets the following basic requirements for enabling
PDCCH and PUSCH resource allocation optimization.
–   Average number of UEs in the cell (L.Traffic.User.Avg) > Value of the
CellUlschAlgo.
UeNumThdInPdcchPuschBal parameter
–   CCE usage > 70%, where CCE usage = (L.ChMeas.CCE.CommUsed +
L.ChMeas.CCE.ULUsed + L.ChMeas.CCE.DLUsed)/L.ChMeas.CCE.Avail
–   Uplink RB usage < 80%, where Uplink RB usage =
L.ChMeas.PRB.UL.Used.Avg/L.ChMeas.PRB.UL.Avail
-  Compare the uplink RB usage within the same period of service time before
and after PDCCH and PUSCH resource allocation optimization is enabled. If
the uplink RB usage increases, this function has taken effect.
## 5.3.3.4.5 Network Monitoring
For details, see 5.3.3.2 Network Analysis.
5.3.4 Optimization on False SR Detection in DRX Mode
## 5.3.4.1 Principles
If an eNodeB falsely detects an SR from a UE (which actually does not send the
SR) and the UE is not in the DRX state, the eNodeB sends an uplink grant. Upon
reception of the uplink grant, the UE sends data or padding as a response
regardless of whether there is a service waiting for data transmission. The eNodeB
monitors the PUSCH to receive data and then responds with ACK or NACK. If the
eNodeB falsely detects an SR from a UE in the DRX state, the eNodeB starts SR
pending for the UE as stipulated in 3GPP specifications and sends an uplink grant
to the UE. In addition, if there is data in the downlink buffer for the UE, the
eNodeB also sends a downlink grant to the UE. However, the UE is in a sleep
period, leading to DTX. Therefore, in DRX scenarios, the
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.159

CellDrxPara.DrxSrDetectOptSwitch parameter can be set to ON to reduce issues
caused by the inconsistency between the eNodeB-maintained UE DRX state and
the actual UE DRX state as a result of false SR detection.
## 5.3.4.2 Network Analysis
## 5.3.4.2.1 Benefits
Optimization of false SR detection in DRX mode reduces the uplink RBLER and
downlink RBLER.
●  Uplink RBLER = (L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.ErrTB.Rbler +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.ErrTB.Rbler + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.ErrTB.Rbler
+ L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.ErrTB.Rbler)/(L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.TB + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.TB)
●  Downlink RBLER = (L.Traffic.DL.SCH.QPSK.ErrTB.Rbler +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.16QAM.ErrTB.Rbler + L.Traffic.DL.SCH.64QAM.ErrTB.Rbler
+ L.Traffic.DL.SCH.256QAM.ErrTB.Rbler)/(L.Traffic.DL.SCH.QPSK.TB +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.16QAM.TB + L.Traffic.DL.SCH.64QAM.TB +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.256QAM.TB)

## 5.3.5 Preallocation
## 5.3.5.1 Principles
During uplink preallocation, an eNodeB proactively sends uplink scheduling
indications to UEs. Uplink preallocation takes effect only when the
CellUlschAlgo.
UlschStrategy parameter is set to ULSCH_STRATEGY_EPF.
Preallocation is also affected by the number of RRC_CONNECTED UEs in the cell.
This function takes effect only when the number of RRC_CONNECTED UEs in the
cell does not exceed the value of the CellUlschAlgo.
PreallocationUserCntThld
parameter.
Preallocation can work in the following modes:
●  Normal mode
●  Smart mode
There are cell- and QCI-level preallocation parameter settings. If available, the
eNodeB preferentially selects the preallocation mode based on QCI-level
preallocation configuration.
Figure 5-2 shows the relationship between the preallocation modes.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.162

Figure 5-2 Relationship between the preallocation modes
To reduce the number of RBs occupied by preallocation, the amount of bandwidth
made available for uplink preallocation can be adapted. This function is controlled
by the CellUlschAlgo.
PrealloSystemBwCoeff parameter. The value of this
parameter ranges from 0 to 10. If this parameter is set to 0, this function is
disabled. If this parameter is set to a value other than 0, the number of RBs
available for uplink preallocation decreases. The larger the parameter value, the
smaller the amount of the decrease.
## Normal Preallocation
Normal preallocation is controlled by the PreAllocationSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter.
Normal preallocation is not subject to the setting of preallocation duration. In
FDD, normal preallocation can be performed within the On Duration period in
DRX mode only when CellDrxPara.
DrxOdPreSchSwitch is set to ON. Other
preallocation modes can take effect only after the option for normal preallocation,
PreAllocationSwitch, is selected. In addition, cell- and QCI-level preallocation
configurations can only take effect if this option is selected.
## Smart Preallocation
Uplink smart preallocation is controlled by the SmartPreAllocationSwitch option
of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter.
Smart preallocation is triggered by downlink services. It cannot be performed
before uplink-initiated TCP services such as web browsing start.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.163

The smart preallocation duration is specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.SmartPreAllocationDuration parameter, and smart preallocation
can be performed in DRX.
The preallocation duration for sparse packets (that is, ping packets) is determined
by the value of either the CellUlschAlgo.SmartPreAllocationDuration parameter
or the CellUlschAlgo.SmartPreAllocDuraForSparse parameter, whichever is
larger.
●  Smart preallocation is disabled if both the
CellUlschAlgo.
SmartPreAllocationDuration and
CellUlschAlgo.
SmartPreAllocDuraForSparse parameters are set to 0.
●  Larger values of the CellUlschAlgo.SmartPreAllocationDuration and
CellUlschAlgo.SmartPreAllocDuraForSparse parameters result in higher UE
power consumption.
## NO TE
Currently, preallocation of sparse-packet services only works for ping services.
To balance throughput and UE power consumption while ensuring that ping
services accurately reflect the packet round-trip delay on live networks, it is
recommended that the CellUlschAlgo.
SmartPreAllocationDuration parameter be
set to a small value (such as 50 ms) and the
CellUlschAlgo.SmartPreAllocDuraForSparse parameter be set to a value greater
than the interval between ping services (such as 1500 ms).
To shorten the smart preallocation duration, adaptation of the uplink smart
preallocation duration can be enabled. This function is controlled by the
CellUlschAlgo.
SmartPrealloDurationCoeff parameter. The value range of this
parameter is 0 to 10. The value 0 indicates that this function is disabled. If this
parameter is set to a value other than 0, the smart preallocation duration is
shortened.
## Cell-level Preallocation Configurations
The total resources made available for preallocation are specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationBandwidthRatio parameter. This parameter
determines the maximum proportion of the total RBs available for preallocation in
a TTI to the system bandwidth.
The amount of data that can be preallocated resources for a UE in a preallocation
queue is specified by the CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationSize parameter.
The amount of data that can be preallocated resources in the smart preallocation
for a UE depends on if the UE is accessing common or sparse-packet services.
●  For common services, the amount is specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationSize parameter.
●  For sparse-packet services, the amount is specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.PreallocationSizeForSparse parameter.
If preallocation is enabled and PUSCH RB resources are still available after UEs
with unsatisfied GBR, UEs with minimum GBR unfulfilled, and UEs with minimum
GBR fulfilled but AMBR unfulfilled are scheduled, then UEs with AMBR unfulfilled
are placed in the preallocation queue when the following conditions are met:
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.164

●  The UEs are not scheduled within the current TTI.
●  The UEs meet the requirement for the minimum interval between
preallocations.
The minimum interval between preallocations is specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationMinPeriod parameter.
If smart preallocation is enabled, the minimum interval between
preallocations for sparse-packet services is specified by the
CellUlschAlgo
.PreAllocMinPeriodForSparse parameter. If this parameter is
set to SAME_AS_OTHER_SERVICE, the minimum interval between
preallocations is determined by the setting of the
CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationMinPeriod parameter.
●  The UE has not undergone semi-persistent scheduling.
●  The UE has a preallocation weight greater than 0.
The preallocation weight is specified by the QciPara.PreAllocationWeight
parameter, which is QCI-specific. The preallocation weight of a UE is
determined by the preallocation weight of the services with the highest-
priority QCI.
The resource preallocation priorities of UEs in the preallocation queue are
determined by the number of preallocations and the preallocation weight. A UE
with fewer preallocations and a greater preallocation weight has a higher priority.
If UEs have the same priority and preallocation weight, the eNodeB selects UEs for
preallocation at random.
QCI-level Preallocation Configurations
In QCI-level preallocation, preallocation parameter groups are configured on a per
QCI basis. A preallocation parameter group includes the configurations of the
preallocation mode, data volume, minimum preallocation interval, and
preallocation duration. Figure 5-3 shows the association between QCIs and
preallocation parameter groups. Each QCI associates with a maximum of one
preallocation parameter group.
Figure 5-3 QCI-level preallocation configurations
The eNodeB traverses the QCIs of all bearers that have been established on a UE.
●  If one or more QCIs are configured with valid PreallocationParaGroupId
values:
The eNodeB preferentially uses QCI-level preallocation configurations for the
## UE.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.165

If multiple QCIs are configured with valid PreallocationParaGroupId values,
the eNodeB selects preallocation configurations for the UE based on a
minimum delay principle. The rules followed are as follows:
–   Rule of preallocation mode selection
The eNodeB preferentially uses the configurations for normal
preallocation. If the configurations for normal preallocation are
unavailable, the eNodeB uses the configurations for smart preallocation.
–   Rule of parameter selection in normal preallocation
If multiple sets of normal preallocation configurations are available, the
minimum preallocation interval equals the minimum one among the
configurations and the data amount for preallocation equals the
maximum one among the configurations.
–   Rule of parameter selection in smart preallocation
If multiple sets of smart preallocation configurations are available, the
minimum preallocation interval equals the minimum one among the
configurations, the data amount for preallocation equals the maximum
one among the configurations, and the smart preallocation duration
equals the maximum one among the configurations.
●  If none of QCIs is configured with a valid value of PreallocationParaGroupId:
The eNodeB uses cell-level preallocation configurations for the UE.
## 5.3.5.2 Network Analysis
## 5.3.5.2.1 Benefits
●  By increasing the number of times the eNodeB proactively schedules UEs,
uplink normal preallocation or uplink smart preallocation reduces the uplink
packet buffering time in the UEs, increases the response speed for UE services,
and improves the service experience.
●  Normal or smart preallocation in the uplink allows the eNodeB to prevent E-
UTRAN radio access bearer (E-RAB) setup signaling reception failures caused
by missing detection of SRs sent from the UEs. That is, normal or smart
preallocation helps increase the E-RAB Setup Success Rate.
●  Adaptation of the uplink smart preallocation duration results in a decreased
proportion of padding for smart preallocation, which increases average uplink
user-perceived throughput. (Average uplink user-perceived throughput =
L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced)
## 5.3.5.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
●  Uplink normal or smart preallocation consumes more Uu interface resources,
which increases the CCE usage and the uplink RB usage. An increased uplink
RB usage results in increased uplink interference.
●  After uplink normal preallocation or uplink smart preallocation is enabled,
uplink IBLER decreases.
●  After uplink normal preallocation or uplink smart preallocation is enabled, the
L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.RmvLastTTI counter value may increase. As a result, User
Uplink Average Throughput decreases.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.166

●  Uplink smart preallocation results in less signaling overhead on the PDCCH
and less uplink interference than when uplink normal preallocation is used.
However, uplink smart preallocation increases uplink delay, and decreases the
web browsing service rate when a small TCP window is configured on the
server.
●  Adaptation of the available bandwidth for uplink preallocation helps reduce
uplink interference, but it causes an increase in uplink delay.

5.3.5.4.2 Using MML Commands
Before using MML commands, confirm the related parameter modification
precautions by referring to the fields including "Service Interrupted After
Modification" and "Caution" for the corresponding parameters in the parameter
reference.
## Activation Command Examples
Before using MML commands, refer to 5.3.5.2.2 Impacts and complete the
parameter configurations for related functions based on the impact relationships
between the functions, as well as the actual network scenario.
//Setting the uplink scheduling policy to EPF
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, UlschStrategy=ULSCH_STRATEGY_EPF;
//Enabling normal preallocation and smart preallocation
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchSwitch=PreAllocationSwitch-1&SmartPreAllocationSwitch-1;
//Enabling QCI-level preallocation
ADD CELLPREALLOCGROUP: LocalCellId=0, PreallocationParaGroupId=0, SmartPreallocationSwitch=ON;
MOD CELLQCIPARA: LocalCellId=0, Qci=9, PreallocationParaGroupId=0;
//Enabling adaptation of the available bandwidth for preallocation
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, PrealloSystemBwCoeff=5;
//Enabling adaptation of the smart preallocation duration
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, SmartPrealloDurationCoeff=6;

## Optimization Command Examples
//Setting parameters related to normal preallocation and smart preallocation
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, PreAllocationBandwidthRatio=25, PreAllocationMinPeriod=5,
PreAllocationSize=80, SmartPreAllocationDuration=50, PreallocationUserCntThld=50;
MOD QCIPARA: Qci=9, PreAllocationWeight=10;
//Setting parameters related to preallocation of sparse-packet services (ping services)
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, SmartPreAllocDuraForSparse=1500,
PreAllocMinPeriodForSparse=SAME_AS_OTHER_SERVICE,
PreallocationSizeForSparse=SAME_AS_OTHER_SERVICE;
//Setting QCI-level preallocation parameters
MOD CELLPREALLOCGROUP: LocalCellId=0, PreallocationParaGroupId=0, PreallocationMinPeriod=5,
PreallocationSize=80, SmartPreallocationDuration=50;
## Deactivation Command Examples
The following provides only deactivation command examples. You can determine
whether to restore the settings of other parameters based on actual network
conditions.
//Disabling normal preallocation and smart preallocation
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchSwitch=PreAllocationSwitch-0&SmartPreAllocationSwitch-0;
5.3.5.4.3 Using the MAE-Deployment
For detailed operations, see Feature Configuration Using the MAE-Deployment.
## 5.3.5.4.4 Activation Verification
## Using Counters
If the value of L.ChMeas.PRB.UL.PreSch.Used.Avg is not 0 after preallocation is
enabled, preallocation has taken effect.
## Using Monitoring Tasks
Use the MAE-Access to verify whether preallocation has taken effect. Perform the
following steps:
-  Enable a UE to access a cell in the cell center. Do not use the UE to perform
uplink services.
-  On the MAE-Access, start UE-level channel quality monitoring (corresponding
to Quality of Channel Monitoring on the GUI) and MCS-based count
monitoring (corresponding to MCS Count Monitoring on the GUI).
-  Check the results of the two monitoring tasks. The channel quality monitoring
task shows the channel quality for the UE, and the MCS-based count
monitoring task shows the number of times the UE is scheduled in the uplink.
If the SINR is approximately 20 dB, and the UE is scheduled 200 times per
second in the uplink after preallocation is enabled, preallocation has taken
effect. (The number of scheduling times is consistent with the default
preallocation period of 5 ms.)
## 5.3.5.4.5 Network Monitoring
For details, see 5.3.5.2 Network Analysis.

5.3.6 Outer-loop DSCP-based Preallocation
## 5.3.6.1 Principles
On the transmission side, operators can use service awareness devices (integrated
into the core network or provided by a third party) to identify services and map
the services to differentiated services code points (DSCPs). DSCPs are classified
into outer-loop DSCPs and inner-loop DSCPs. For details, see
## Video Experience
## Optimization
. The eNodeB can obtain the outer-loop DSCP value of a service only
when the OUTER_LOOP_DSCP_QOS_MGMT_SW option of the
CellAlgoExtSwitch.
DlSchEnhSwitch parameter is selected. In addition, the
QciPara.OLDscpTransparentTransSw parameter must be set to ON to ensure
that outer-loop DSCP values can be transparently transmitted to the PDCP layer of
the eNodeB.
Based on the obtained outer-loop DSCP values, the eNodeB can perform uplink
preallocation for UEs running specific services. This function requires that both the
DSCP_PREALLOCATION_SW option of the CellAlgoExtSwitch.
UlSchEnhSwitch
parameter and the PREALLOCATION_SW option of the
IpServiceQos.
ApplicationRange parameter be selected. In this case, the data
volume that can be preallocated resources for UEs running such services is
determined by the CellUlschAlgo.
SplUePreallocDataVolume parameter, and the
minimum preallocation interval is determined by the
CellUlschAlgo.SplUePreallocMinInterval parameter.
Outer-loop DSCP-based preallocation takes effect only when normal preallocation
is enabled. When smart preallocation is enabled, smart preallocation also takes
effect for these UEs for a period specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.
SplUeSmartPreallocDuration parameter.
Preallocation for these UEs is not affected by the
CellUlschAlgo.
PreallocationUserCntThld and
CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationBandwidthRatio parameters.
●  Preallocation can still take effect for these UEs running specific services when
the number of UEs in RRC_CONNECTED mode in a cell exceeds the value of
the CellUlschAlgo.
PreallocationUserCntThld parameter.
●  If the ratio of the number of preallocated RBs in the current TTI to the
number of RBs in the cell bandwidth exceeds the value of the
CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationBandwidthRatio parameter, preallocation can
still take effect for these UEs running specific services.
## 5.3.6.2 Network Analysis
## 5.3.6.2.1 Benefits
Outer-loop DSCP-based preallocation increases the number of times the eNodeB
proactively schedules UEs running specific services. This reduces the buffer
duration of uplink data packets for such UEs and improves user experience.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.173

## 5.3.6.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
Outer-loop DSCP-based preallocation increases air interface resource consumption,
CCE usage, and uplink RB usage. An increased uplink RB usage results in increased
uplink interference.

## Activation Command Examples
Before using MML commands, refer to 5.3.6.3.2 Software and complete the
parameter configurations for related functions based on the dependency
relationships between the functions, as well as the actual network scenario.
//Enabling QoS management based on outer-loop DSCP
MOD CELLALGOEXTSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, DlSchEnhSwitch=OUTER_LOOP_DSCP_QOS_MGMT_SW-1;

MOD CELLALGOEXTSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchEnhSwitch=DSCP_PREALLOCATION_SW-1;

ApplicationRange=PREALLOCATION_SW-1;
