MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, SplUePreallocDataVolume=80, SplUePreallocMinInterval=5,
SplUeSmartPreallocDuration=50;

5.3.7 Scheduling Restriction on Abnormal UEs
## 5.3.7.1 Principles
Scheduling Restriction on UEs with Abnormal Uplink Padding
When a UE sends a large amount of uplink padding data, the MAC layer of the UE
sends a large amount of data, but the throughput at the PDCP layer does not
change significantly. Such a UE is called a UE with abnormal uplink padding.
UEs with abnormal uplink padding preempt a large number of uplink RBs,
affecting the uplink rates of normal UEs. The function of scheduling restriction on
UEs with abnormal uplink padding periodically identifies UEs with abnormal
uplink padding and restricts the number of RBs that can be scheduled for these
UEs. This function is controlled by the ABN_UE_SCH_RESTRICTION_SW option of
the CellAlgoExtSwitch.
UlSchEnhSwitch parameter.
●Identification of abnormal UEs: Based on UEs' BSRs and proportions of
abnormal padding data, the eNodeB periodically identifies the UEs with
abnormal uplink padding.
–   If the CellUlschAlgo.
AbnormalUeSchCntThld parameter is set to 0, the
eNodeB periodically identifies any UE with abnormal uplink padding
based on UEs' PRB usages and proportions of abnormal padding data.
–   If the CellUlschAlgo.
AbnormalUeSchCntThld parameter is set to a value
other than 0, the eNodeB checks the expected number of RBs to be
scheduled for a UE and the proportion of abnormal padding data to
identify whether the UE has abnormal padding. If the eNodeB determines
that the padding of a UE is abnormal for a consecutive number of times
specified by the CellUlschAlgo.
AbnormalUeSchCntThld parameter, the
eNodeB identifies the UE as a UE with abnormal uplink padding.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.177

●  Scheduling restriction on abnormal UEs: The eNodeB restricts within a fixed
period the number of RBs that can be scheduled for identified UEs with
abnormal uplink padding. This prevents abnormal UEs from occupying excess
RBs and increases the average uplink data rate of UEs in a cell. If the
CellUlschAlgo.AbnormalUeSchCntThld parameter is set to a value other
than 0 and the proportion of abnormal padding data of a UE becomes
normal within a fixed period of time, the eNodeB cancels the scheduling
restriction on the UE.
Scheduling Restriction for UEs with Abnormal SRs or SRB BSRs
This function is controlled by the ABN_UE_SCH_RESTRICTION_SW2 option of the
CellAlgoExtSwitch.
UlSchEnhSwitch parameter.
●  Abnormal UE identification: The eNodeB identifies a UE (excluding eMTC UEs)
as one with abnormal SRs or SRB BSRs in any of the following scenarios:
–   The UE continuously reports SRs but has no uplink data.
–   The UE continuously reports SRB BSRs but does not have uplink SRB data.
–   The UE continuously reports SRB BSRs indicating a relatively large buffer
size but has a relatively low proportion of valid uplink SRB data volume
to the total valid uplink data volume.
●  Scheduling restriction on abnormal UEs: The eNodeB restricts the number of
RBs that can be scheduled for identified UEs with abnormal SRs or SRB BSRs
and lowers the scheduling priorities of these UEs.
## NO TE
Given that SRBs belong to logical channel group (LCG) 0, a buffer status report in which
the LCG ID is 0 is referred to as an SRB BSR in this document.
## 5.3.7.2 Network Analysis
## 5.3.7.2.1 Benefits
●  Scheduling restriction on UEs with abnormal uplink padding
Scheduling restriction on UEs with abnormal uplink padding identifies UEs
with abnormal uplink padding and restricts the number of RBs that can be
scheduled for such UEs, ensuring RB resources for normal UEs and increasing
the average uplink rate of UEs in the cell.
You are advised to enable this function for cells meeting the following
conditions. If any of the conditions is not met, there will be limited benefits,
but no negative impacts.
–   There is a sharp increase in the uplink PRB usage for a period of time (for
example, half an hour).
–   The average uplink rate of UEs in the cell decreases.
●  Scheduling restriction on UEs with abnormal SRs or SRB BSRs
–   If there are abnormal UEs that continuously report SRs but have no
uplink data, this function prevents these UEs from occupying excess RB
resources to increase the average uplink rate of UEs.
–   If there are abnormal UEs that continuously report SRB BSRs but have no
uplink SRB data or that continuously report SRB BSRs indicating a
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.178

relatively large buffer size but have a relatively low proportion of valid
uplink SRB data volume to total valid uplink data volume, this function
ensures resource scheduling fairness.
## 5.3.7.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
●  Scheduling restriction on UEs with abnormal uplink padding
## None
●  Scheduling restriction on UEs with abnormal SRs or SRB BSRs
–   If there are abnormal UEs that continuously report SRs but have no
uplink data, the random access success rate may deteriorate, and
indicators such as the uplink BLER, PRB usage, RB usage, number of
scheduled UEs per TTI (SUNPT), or uplink traffic volume may fluctuate.
–   If there are abnormal UEs that continuously report SRB BSRs but have no
uplink SRB data or that continuously report SRB BSRs indicating a
relatively large buffer size but have a relatively low proportion of valid
uplink SRB data volume to total valid uplink data volume, the uplink
throughput of these UEs decreases and the random access success rate
may deteriorate. In addition, indicators such as the uplink BLER, PRB
usage, RB usage, SUNPT, or uplink traffic volume may fluctuate.
## Function Impacts
## None

5.3.8 Preallocation for NSA DC UEs (FDD)
## 5.3.8.1 Principles
Uplink preallocation is restricted by the number of UEs in RRC_CONNECTED mode
in a cell. In the event of using non-standalone (NSA) networking during LTE-to-5G
evolution, the UE number threshold for performing preallocation for NSA DC UEs
is separately specified by the NsaDcMgmtConfig.
NsaDcUePreUserCntThld
parameter to ensure that uplink preallocation can be performed for more NSA DC
UEs.
●  If this parameter is set to 0 for a cell, preallocation for NSA DC UEs in the cell
is the same as that for non-NSA DC UEs. For details, see 5.3.5 Preallocation.
●  When this parameter is set to a non-zero value:
–   If the number of UEs in RRC_CONNECTED mode in a cell exceeds the
specified threshold, the eNodeB does not perform preallocation for NSA
DC UEs. For details about preallocation for non-NSA DC UEs, see 5.3.5
## Preallocation.
–   If the number of UEs in RRC_CONNECTED mode in a cell does not exceed
the threshold, the eNodeB performs preallocation for NSA DC UEs and
non-NSA DC UEs. For details, see 5.3.5 Preallocation. However,
preallocation for NSA DC UEs has the following differences:
## ▪
The cell-level common preallocation switch and the UE number
threshold for preallocation do not take effect for NSA DC UEs.
Specifically, when the SmartPreAllocationSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter is selected, smart
preallocation is performed for NSA DC UEs. If this option is
deselected, normal preallocation is performed for NSA DC UEs.
## ▪
When normal preallocation takes effect for UEs in DRX state, normal
preallocation in the On Duration period can be performed for NSA
DC UEs but is not available for non-NSA DC UEs unless the
CellDrxPara.
DrxOdPreSchSwitch parameter is set to ON.
## 5.3.8.2 Network Analysis
## 5.3.8.2.1 Benefits
By increasing the number of times that the base station proactively schedules UEs,
preallocation for NSA DC UEs reduces the uplink data packet buffering time in the
UEs, increases the response speed for UE services, and improves the service
experience.
## 5.3.8.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
## None

5.3.8.4.2 Using MML Commands
Before using MML commands, confirm the related parameter modification
precautions by referring to the fields including "Service Interrupted After
Modification" and "Caution" for the corresponding parameters in the parameter
reference.
## Activation Command Examples
//Setting the UE number threshold in preallocation for NSA DC UEs to a non-zero value
MOD NSADCMGMTCONFIG: LocalCellId=0, NsaDcUePreUserCntThld=100;
//Enabling smart preallocation for NSA DC UEs
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchSwitch=SmartPreAllocationSwitch-1;
//Enabling normal preallocation for non-NSA DC UEs in the On Duration period of DRX
MOD CELLDRXPARA: LocalCellId=0, DrxOdPreSchSwitch=ON;
## Deactivation Command Examples
//Setting the UE number threshold in preallocation for NSA DC UEs to 0
MOD NSADCMGMTCONFIG: LocalCellId=0, NsaDcUePreUserCntThld=0;
//Enabling normal preallocation for NSA DC UEs
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchSwitch=SmartPreAllocationSwitch-0;

5.3.9 Uplink Short-Interval SPS (FDD)
## 5.3.9.1 Principles
A UE must obtain an uplink grant before it can transmit data in the uplink. If a
relatively long time is required to obtain the uplink grant, uplink short-interval SPS
can be used for UEs that are running services requiring low latency to reduce the
UE transmission delay for the first uplink packet. Services with a specific QCI
require low latency if the CellQciPara.
LowLatencyFlag parameter is set to TRUE
for this QCI.
The interval of uplink short-interval SPS is specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.
IntvlOfUlSpsWithSkipping parameter.
●  When this parameter is set to a value other than MS0, short-interval SPS is
performed at the specified interval using the TBS specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.
TbsForUlSpsWithSkipping parameter.
●  When this parameter is set to MS0, the function does not take effect.
## 5.3.9.2 Network Analysis
## 5.3.9.2.1 Benefits
In uplink light load scenarios (for example, the number of UEs is less than 100 and
the PUSCH RB usage is less than 30%), uplink short-interval SPS reduces the
transmission delay for the first uplink packet of related UEs.
## 5.3.9.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
●  UEs that have entered uplink short-interval SPS occupy fixed uplink resources,
which increases the uplink RB usage and decreases the number of uplink RBs
for other UEs. As a result, Cell Uplink Average Throughput decreases.
●  Uplink short-interval SPS for some UEs postpones the uplink feedback to
downlink services of other UEs. As a result, Cell Downlink Average
Throughput decreases.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.185

●  When UEs that have entered uplink short-interval SPS quickly move to a weak
coverage area, the eNodeB may fail to immediately identify whether the UEs
are in the skipping state or uplink data transmission state. As a result, the
Service Drop Rate increases.

5.4 UE Service Priorities in Uplink Scheduling
Schedulers use the EPF scheduling algorithm to meet end-to-end QoS
requirements. Unlike downlink QoS guarantee, uplink QoS is guaranteed by using
related parameters on the eNodeB and related parameters sent from the eNodeB
to UEs. The eNodeB can obtain accurate information about the data volumes of
downlink services and perform downlink scheduling based on the data volumes
and service priorities. However, the eNodeB cannot obtain accurate information
about the data volumes of uplink services. Therefore, the eNodeB performs uplink
scheduling based on the scheduling priority of each UE. The uplink services of
each scheduled UE are then scheduled based on their data volumes and service
priorities.
UE services can be classified as either GBR services or non-GBR services. When
both GBR and non-GBR services are running in a cell, the scheduler preferentially
guarantees the rates of GBR services, and adopts a best-effort policy for non-GBR
services with a minimum bit rate guaranteed. Scenario-specific conditions must
also be considered for scheduling priority calculation and service rate guarantee.
This section describes the following related functions:
●  Uplink GBR and non-GBR service priority calculation
●  Uplink scheduling based on the maximum bit rate
●  MBR-based uplink rate control
●  Uplink traffic-model-based performance optimization
●  Optimization of uplink scheduling priority accuracy
●  Uplink scheduling duration optimization
●  Rate Guarantee Optimization for Uplink Non-GBR Services
5.4.1 Uplink GBR and Non-GBR Service Priority Calculation
## 5.4.1.1 Principles
UE Parameters for Uplink QoS
Uplink QoS is guaranteed using related parameters on the eNodeB and those that
the eNodeB sends to the UEs. The parameters for uplink QoS on the UE side are
as follows:
●  Prioritized bit rate (PBR)
In accordance with 3GPP specifications, a PBR must be preferentially ensured
on uplink logical channels. The PBR ensures that UEs on low-priority logical
channels are scheduled when resource congestion occurs, preventing the
service rate from dropping to zero on such channels due to resource
congestion. The value range of the PBR for logical channels is specified in
section 6.3.2 in 3GPP TS 36.331 V10.1.0 (2011-03).
eRAN

The configuration suggestions and details are as follows:
–   A larger value for the logical channel priority indicates a lower scheduling
priority.
–   To meet the QoS requirements of GBR services and signaling with QCI 5:
## ▪
Set the logical channel priority for GBR services and signaling with

## ▪
Set the logical channel priority for non-GBR services to a value from
11 to 15.

15, or 16.
●  Logical channel group
A UE can report the total amount of buffered data for multiple services with
different QCIs, but not for each individual service. This reduces consumption
of system resources. These services must be mapped to the same logical
channel group. The eNodeB supports four logical channel groups. The
GlobalProcSwitch.LcgProfile parameter specifies logical channel group
profiles. The following describes the parameter values and grouping
principles:
## –   LCG_PROFILE_0
An eNodeB assigns only one logical channel group to non-GBR services.
The logical channel groups are listed in Table 5-20.

Non-GBR Service Priority Calculation
The uplink scheduler uses a best-effort policy for non-GBR service transmission,
and ensures that the total rate of all non-GBR services of a UE does not exceed
the AMBR configured in the EPC. When the UlMinGbrSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter is selected, the eNodeB can identify UEs
by QCI or SPID and set an uplink minimum GBR for specific UEs.
●  QCI-based identification: Operators can specify different QCIs for default non-
GBR bearers of different UE types on the EPC. An uplink minimum GBR for
bearers with different QCIs can be configured using the
CellQciParaExtension.
UlMinGbr parameter on the eNodeB.
●  SPID-based identification: Operators can specify different SPIDs for different
UE types on the EPC. An uplink minimum GBR corresponding to the default
non-GBR bearers of UEs with a specified SPID can be configured using the
SpidCfg.
UlMinGbr parameter on the eNodeB.
For a specific UE, the eNodeB uses the value of the SpidCfg.UlMinGbr parameter
as the uplink minimum GBR if it is a non-zero value; otherwise, the eNodeB uses
the value of the CellQciParaExtension.UlMinGbr parameter.
●  When Uu resources are sufficient:
–   If the minimum GBR of non-GBR services does not exceed the PBR, the
service rate can reach the minimum GBR.
–   If the minimum GBR of non-GBR services exceeds the PBR, the service
rate may not reach the minimum GBR when multiple bearers are set up,
especially for low-priority non-GBR services. This is because UEs
guarantee the PBRs in descending order of logical-channel priorities.
●  When Uu resources are insufficient:
–   If both GBR and non-GBR services are running in the cell, GBR services
are preferentially scheduled. This means that the non-GBR service rates
may not reach the minimum GBR.
–   If only non-GBR services are running in the cell and the amount of uplink
resources required to meet the minimum GBR of a UE exceeds the
amount of resources provided by the system, the non-GBR service rate of
the UE may not reach its minimum GBR.
The eNodeB uses a token bucket algorithm to control the uplink service rate. The
size of the token bucket affects the guaranteed rate for services and the service
rate determines the rate at which tokens are added to the bucket. The number of
tokens in the bucket indicates whether the service rate meets requirements.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.196

The priority is then calculated by using the following formula:
where
● indicates the current channel quality of the UE.
## ●
indicates the historical transmission rate of the UE.
● indicates the QCI-specific weight factor for the scheduling priority of the
service. This factor can be set using the
CellQciParaExtension.
UlSchPriorityWeightFactor parameter. A larger weight
factor indicates a higher scheduling priority.
● indicates the weight coefficient for the uplink scheduling priority
corresponding to the SPID of the UE. This weight coefficient is specified by the
SpidCfg.
UlSchPriCoeff parameter and is valid only when the eNodeB
identifies that the UE is configured with an SPID. For details, see WBB.
## ●
indicates the capacity factor for EPF. This factor can be set using the
CellUlschAlgo.UlEpfCapacityFactor parameter.
–   When the factor is set to 1, the scheduling priority selection is oriented
towards resource fairness.
–   When the factor is set to a value smaller than 1, the scheduling priority
selection is oriented towards rate fairness.
–   When the factor is set to a value greater than 1, the scheduling priority
selection is oriented towards capacity.
GBR Service Priority Calculation
The uplink scheduler considers the channel quality and historical transmission rate
of a UE when calculating the GBR service priority for the UE and preferentially
guarantees the rate of GBR services.
## 5.4.1.2 Network Analysis
## 5.4.1.2.1 Benefits
GBR and non-GBR service priority calculation guarantees the QoS for services of
various QCIs and provides differentiated services.
## 5.4.1.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
For the logical channel priority settings of QCI 1 to QCI 5:
●  If the logical channel priority of QCI 1 is lower than that of QCI 2 to QCI 4,
the Uplink Packet Loss Rate (VoIP) may increase in hybrid services of QCI 1
and QCI 2 to QCI 4.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.197

●  If the logical channel priority of QCI 1 is higher than that of QCI 5, the
scheduling delay for QCI-5 bearers may slightly increase in multi-bearer
service scenarios with QCI 1 and QCI 5.
●  If the logical channel priority of QCI 1 is higher than that of QCI 5 and the
preallocation weight for QCI 1 is 0 (indicating that preallocation is disabled
when the bearer for QCI 1 is available), the Uplink Packet Loss Rate (VoIP)
may increase due to missing detection of SRs.
In minimum GBR configuration for non-GBR services:
●  Given that the eNodeB preferentially guarantees the rates of UEs configured
with minimum GBR, available resources decrease for non-GBR UEs without
such configuration, possibly causing Service Uplink Average Throughput to
decrease for such UEs.
●  When most UEs configured with uplink minimum GBR are located at the cell
edge, the eNodeB uses excessive resources to guarantee the rates of these
UEs, possibly causing Cell Uplink Average Throughput to decrease.
●  When most UEs configured with uplink minimum GBR are located near the
cell center, the eNodeB uses excessive resources to guarantee the rates of
these UEs, possibly causing Cell Uplink Average Throughput to increase.

## 5.4.1.4.4 Activation Verification
The following example shows how to observe whether the uplink minimum GBR
of UEs takes effect when the UlMinGbrSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchSwitch parameter is selected.
-  Enable two cell center UEs (UE1 and UE2) with the same channel conditions
to access a 5 MHz cell and set up non-GBR bearers. The bearer of UE 1 uses
QCI 8, with CellQciParaExtension.
UlMinGbr set to MinGbrRate_1536_KB.
The bearer of UE 2 uses QCI 9, with CellQciParaExtension.UlMinGbr set to
MinGbrRate_0_KB.
2.Confirm that the SchedulerCtrlPowerSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchSwitch parameter is deselected. Then, perform uplink
full buffer services for the two UEs at the same time.
-  Check the UL Used RB Num values for the two UEs on the MAE-Access to
obtain the number of RBs used by each UE before and after the
UlMinGbrSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter is
selected. Before the option is selected, the ratio between the numbers of RBs
used by the two UEs is close to 1:1. After the option is selected, the
percentage of RBs used by UE 1 increases, indicating that this function has
taken effect.
## 5.4.1.4.5 Network Monitoring
For details, see 5.4.1.2 Network Analysis.
5.4.2 Uplink Scheduling Based on the Maximum Bit Rate
## 5.4.2.1 Principles
In the EPC, a UE-AMBR is set for the total rate of all uplink non-GBR services and
an MBR is set for the total rate of all uplink GBR services for each UE.
When different UEs in a cell have different UE-AMBRs:
●  If the UeAmbrUlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch
parameter is deselected, the eNodeB does not consider UE-AMBRs when
calculating scheduling priorities.
●  If the UeAmbrUlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch
parameter is selected, the eNodeB considers UE-AMBRs when calculating
scheduling priorities to implement differentiated scheduling of non-GBR
services with different service rate subscriptions.
When different UEs in a cell have different MBRs:
●  If the MbrUlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter
is deselected, the eNodeB does not consider MBRs when calculating
scheduling priorities.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.201

●  If the MbrUlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchSwitch parameter
is selected, the eNodeB considers MBRs when calculating scheduling priorities
to implement differentiated scheduling of GBR services with different service
rate subscriptions.
## 5.4.2.2 Network Analysis
## 5.4.2.2.1 Benefits
●  When the UeAmbrUlSchSwitch option is selected, UEs with high UE-AMBRs
are preferentially allocated resources during scheduling. The average uplink
UE throughput increases for such UEs.
●  When the MbrUlSchSwitch option is selected, UEs with high MBRs are
preferentially allocated resources during scheduling. The average uplink UE
throughput increases for such UEs.
## 5.4.2.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
●  If the UeAmbrUlSchSwitch option is selected when the system is congested,
UEs with high AMBRs will occupy a large amount of resources, which may
cause Cell Uplink Average Throughput to decrease.
●  If the MbrUlSchSwitch option is selected when the system is congested, UEs
with high MBRs will occupy a large amount of resources, which may cause
Cell Uplink Average Throughput to decrease.

-  Enable UE 1, UE 2, and UE 3 with the same channel conditions and an AMBR

UeAmbrUlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.

5.4.3 MBR-based Uplink Rate Control
## 5.4.3.1 Principles
The MBR is a QoS parameter set in the EPC for GBR services. Generally, the MBR
must be greater than or equal to the GBR. When the scheduler schedules GBR
services, it determines whether to consider the MBR for uplink rate control based
on the setting of the UlMbrCtrlSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch
parameter.
●  If the UlMbrCtrlSwitch option is deselected, the uplink scheduler uses a best-
effort policy to allocate air interface resources to ensure that the total rate of
GBR services on the UE reaches but does not exceed the total GBR of all GBR
services.
●  If the UlMbrCtrlSwitch option is selected, the uplink scheduler uses a best-
effort policy to allocate air interface resources to ensure that the total rate of
GBR services on the UE reaches but does not exceed the total MBR of all GBR
services.
## 5.4.3.2 Network Analysis
## 5.4.3.2.1 Benefits
If the UlMbrCtrlSwitch option is selected, GBR services can be allocated more
resources. The average uplink service rate of GBR services increases and can
exceed the GBR, with the MBR as the upper limit.
## 5.4.3.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
GBR bearers consume more resources when the system is congested. As a result,
fewer resources are available for non-GBR bearers, and the average uplink service
throughput on the non-GBR bearers decreases.

5.4.4 Uplink Traffic-Model-based Performance Optimization
## 5.4.4.1 Principles
On live networks, traffic models differ greatly depending on the UEs. The
scheduler considers traffic models as well as signal quality during scheduling. It
preferentially schedules UEs with smaller amount of service buffer data and better
signal quality. This function is controlled by the UlPacketLenAwareSchSw option
of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchExtSwitch parameter.
## 5.4.4.2 Network Analysis
## 5.4.4.2.1 Benefits
In scenarios with a heavy traffic load (for example, with the number of UEs
greater than 150 and the PUSCH RB usage greater than 60%), uplink traffic-
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.207

model-based scheduling reduces service wait time for UEs, allows higher-order
uplink MCSs, and increases average uplink user-perceived throughput by 5% to
20% while basically maintaining cell capacity. In scenarios with a light traffic load,
this function increases average uplink user-perceived throughput less significantly
but does not bring additional negative gains. (Average uplink user-perceived
throughput = L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced)

●  Fast batch activation

## 5.4.4.4.4 Activation Verification
Step 1Check the number of UEs and RB usage in the cell. It is recommended that you
verify the function activation when there are more than 150 UEs in the cell and
the PUSCH RB usage of the cell is over 60%.
Step 2Adjust service configurations. It is recommended that the average data rate be
approximately 8 Mbit/s for large-packet services and 12 kbit/s for small-packet
services and that the ratio of UEs running large-packet services to UEs running
small-packet services be 1:4.
Step 3Check the average uplink user-perceived throughput after the function has been
activated for a while. If the throughput increases by 5% or higher, the function has
taken effect.
----End
## 5.4.4.4.5 Network Monitoring
●  Average uplink user-perceived throughput = L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/
L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced
●  PUSCH RB usage = L.ChMeas.PRB.PUSCH.Avg/
L.ChMeas.PRB.UL.PUSCH.Avail
5.4.5 Optimization of Uplink Scheduling Priority Accuracy
## 5.4.5.1 Principles
When the EPF or PF scheduling policy is used, the formula for uplink scheduling
priority calculation uses a filtered rate as the denominator. The filtered rate is
calculated using the fixed-point calculation method. This calculation method
rounds down the filtered rate (a division result) to the nearest integer and
therefore reduces the accuracy of the filtered rate. If the uplink rate of a UE is very
low, it is possible that the filtered rate will be rounded down to 0. This will result
in abnormal scheduling priority calculation and scheduling differentiation will be
affected. When the UlSchRbAllocOptSw option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchExtSwitch parameter is selected, the eNodeB rounds off the
filtered value either up or down to make the calculation result more accurate.
With this parameter setting, the eNodeB also prevents the filtered value from
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.210

being rounded down to zero, ensuring that the scheduling priority can be
calculated without any abnormalities. Such optimization ensures scheduling
differentiation effects.
## 5.4.5.2 Network Analysis
## 5.4.5.2.1 Benefits
Scheduling priorities can be calculated more accurately when all the following
conditions are met:
●  Average uplink MCS index ≤ 4
●  Uplink PRB Usage > 80%
●  CCE usage > 70%
●  Number of UEs in a cell > 300
Therefore, UEs in the cell center or a medium distance from the cell center have
more scheduling opportunities, increasing Cell Uplink Average Throughput. In
addition, considering that UEs with different weights generally have similar
channel qualities in most cases, enabling this optimization function in the event of
a large scheduling weight difference allows UEs with a higher weight to be
allocated more RBs than UEs with a lower weight, which magnifies the
differentiation effect. When the preceding conditions are not met, scheduling
priorities are calculated normally and therefore this function produces a limited
optimization effect. However, it does not bring additional negative impacts on
uplink performance.
## 5.4.5.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
In the scenario described in 5.4.5.2.1 Benefits, the increase in the uplink
scheduling opportunities for UEs in the cell center and UEs at a medium distance
from the cell center reduces the number of uplink-synchronized UEs in the cell and
decreases the uplink IBLER. In addition, as the number of scheduling opportunities
changes in the uplink, the number of scheduling opportunities also changes in the
downlink. Therefore, the downlink performance, for example the average downlink
MCS index, will be affected. The performance change trend is strongly correlated
with the number of downlink scheduling times and the physical distribution of the
UEs within the cell.
## Function Impacts
## None

## 5.4.5.3.1 Licenses

5.4.6 Uplink Scheduling Duration Optimization (FDD)
## 5.4.6.1 Principles
When there are a large number of UEs, the uplink scheduler, having to balance
the CPU load and uplink performance, periodically updates the scheduling
information of some UEs to be scheduled to reduce the CPU load. If there is
buffered data for a UE to be scheduled, the scheduling information may not be
promptly updated, prolonging the scheduling delay and affecting the average
uplink user-perceived throughput. Uplink scheduling duration optimization can be
enabled using the UL_SCH_DURATION_OPT_SW option of the
CellAlgoExtSwitch.
UlSchEnhSwitch parameter to update scheduling information
about to-be scheduled UEs with buffered data in a more timely manner, thereby
improving the average uplink user-perceived throughput.
## 5.4.6.2 Network Analysis
## 5.4.6.2.1 Benefits
The uplink scheduling duration optimization function improves the average uplink
user-perceived throughput (L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/
L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced) when both of the following conditions are met:
●  Uplink PRB usage < 40%
●  Average number of UEs served by a BBP > 300

5.4.7 Rate Guarantee Optimization for Uplink Non-GBR
## Services
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.215

## 5.4.7.1 Principles
In heavy-load scenarios, the minimum GBR (specified by the
CellQciParaExtension.UlMinGbr or SpidCfg.UlMinGbr parameter) configured for
non-GBR services may not be reached. In this case, the rate guarantee
optimization function for uplink non-GBR services can be used to increase the
scheduling priorities of UEs running services with the related QCI, ensuring the
uplink rate experience of UEs configured with the minimum GBR. This function
can be enabled by selecting the RATE_GUARANTEE_FLAG option of the
QciPara.
AggregationAttribute parameter and specifying the proportion of
resources available for uplink rate guarantee
(CellUlschAlgo.
UlRateGuaranteeResRatio).
When multiple bearers are set up for a UE running guaranteed services:
●  If the bearers are for non-GBR services and configured with the
CellQciParaExtension.
UlMinGbr parameter, the eNodeB guarantees the
uplink rate of the UE based on the sum of the
CellQciParaExtension.UlMinGbr values of all bearers.
●  If both the bearers for non-GBR services and those for GBR services exist, the
eNodeB guarantees the uplink rate of the UE based on the sum of the
CellQciParaExtension.
UlMinGbr parameter for all non-GBR bearers and the
PBR for GBR bearers.
## 5.4.7.2 Network Analysis
## 5.4.7.2.1 Benefits
In heavy-load scenarios (for example, when the number of UEs on a BBP is greater
than 800), rate guarantee optimization for uplink non-GBR services increases User
Uplink Average Throughput of UEs running non-GBR services to be guaranteed.
## 5.4.7.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
When uplink RBs or CCEs are insufficient, User Uplink Average Throughput
decreases for UEs performing non-GBR services without rate guarantee.

5.5 UE Data Amount Calculation for Uplink Scheduling
## 5.5.1 Principles
The eNodeB needs to estimate the amount of data to be scheduled for a UE that
has sent an SR. (This type of UE is referred to as an SR UE.)
If the number of retransmission attempts of an SR UE has reached the maximum
allowed and all of the retransmission attempts have failed, the SR UE cannot send
BSRs and therefore scheduling cannot be started for the UE. In this situation, the
eNodeB attempts to schedule the SR UE again and performs the attempt twice.
This process is referred to as re-scheduling of SR UEs. If PUSCH DTX is detected in
initial transmission from an SR UE, the eNodeB stops re-scheduling the SR UE.
The UlEnhancedSrSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch
parameter specifies whether to optimize uplink re-scheduling of SR UEs. When this
option is selected, re-scheduling of SR UEs in DRX mode is performed only when
the On Duration Timer is running. For details about the On Duration Timer, see
DRX and Signaling Control.
The amount of data to be scheduled in SR-based scheduling is determined by
related parameters as follows:
●  If the CellUlschAlgo.
UlSrSchDataVolAdptOptUpThd parameter is set to a
non-zero value, the following two scenarios exist (for an FDD cell, such
setting takes effect only if the cell is not established on the LBBPc):
–   If the CellUlschAlgo.UlSrSchDataVolAdptOptUpThd parameter is set to
a value greater than the value of the CellUlschAlgo.
UlSrSchDateLen
parameter, the amount is calculated based on the amount of data
scheduled during the last SR-based scheduling and falls into the
following range: [CellUlschAlgo.
UlSrSchDateLen,
CellUlschAlgo.
UlSrSchDataVolAdptOptUpThd].
–   If the CellUlschAlgo.
UlSrSchDataVolAdptOptUpThd parameter is set to
a value less than or equal to the value of the
CellUlschAlgo.
UlSrSchDateLen parameter, the eNodeB uses the
CellUlschAlgo.
UlSrSchDateLen parameter value as the amount of data
to be scheduled.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.219

●  If the CellUlschAlgo.UlSrSchDataVolAdptOptUpThd parameter is set to 0,
the eNodeB determines the amount as follows:
–   The eNodeB uses the CellUlschAlgo.UlSrSchDateLen parameter value as
the specific amount of data when a certain condition is met. For FDD, the
condition is that the SrSchDataAdptSw option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter is deselected.
–   The eNodeB uses the CellUlschAlgo.UlSrSchDateLen parameter value as
the minimum amount of data to be scheduled and adaptively determines
the final amount of data based on the previous amount of data
scheduled during the last SR-based scheduling when a certain condition
is met. If the previous amount of data is greater than 700 bits, the
amount can be used to calculate the amount of data to be scheduled in
the current SR-based scheduling. The maximum amount of data to be
scheduled in SR-based scheduling is always 16,550 bits. For FDD, the
condition is that the SrSchDataAdptSw option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter is selected.
## 5.5.2 Network Analysis
## 5.5.2.1 Benefits
For FDD, when data amount adaptation in SR-based scheduling is enabled:
●  If the uplink RB usage is less than or equal to 90%, this function increases
average uplink user-perceived throughput. (Average uplink user-perceived
throughput = L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/
L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced)
●  If the uplink RB usage is greater than 90% and the
CellUlschAlgo.
UlSrSchDataVolAdptOptUpThd parameter is set to 2000, this
function increases the uplink traffic volume and Cell Uplink Average
## Throughput.

5.6 RB Quantity Calculation for Uplink Scheduling
## 5.6.1 Scheduler-controlled Power
## 5.6.1.1 Principles
The power control module of an eNodeB sets a target value for convergence of
the power spectral density (PSD) for each UE to maintain the maximum number
of allocatable RBs under the PSD. This maximum number of allocatable RBs is
called the number of power-limited RBs. The SchedulerCtrlPowerSwitch option
of the CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchSwitch parameter determines whether the number
of RBs allocated by the scheduler can be greater than the number of power-
limited RBs.
●  If the SchedulerCtrlPowerSwitch option is deselected, the number of RBs
allocated by the scheduler cannot exceed the number of power-limited RBs.
The PSD remains at the level limited by power control.
●  If the SchedulerCtrlPowerSwitch option is selected, the number of RBs
allocated by the scheduler can exceed the number of power-limited RBs.
If the number of RBs allocated to a UE exceeds the number of power-limited RBs,
the PSD for the UE's data transmission is lower than the power-control-limited
PSD because of the maximum UE transmit power restriction, which causes a
decreased SINR. However, the scheduler maintains the actual SINR to a level that
will not decrease the UE throughput.
## 5.6.1.2 Network Analysis
## 5.6.1.2.1 Benefits
If the scheduler-controlled power function is enabled, the MCS index selected for
uplink transmission and average uplink user-perceived throughput increase in
scenarios where the uplink RB usage is low and uplink interference is light.
(Average uplink user-perceived throughput = L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/
L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced)
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.223

## 5.6.1.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
After the scheduler-controlled power function is enabled, Cell Uplink Average
Throughput decreases because fewer UEs are scheduled if the following
conditions are met:
●  The PUSCH RB usage is greater than 80%.
●  The average number of UEs scheduled in the uplink per TTI (specified by
L.Traffic.SUNPT.UL.Avg) is greater than 5.

5.6.2 Optimized Uplink RLC Segment Reduction
## 5.6.2.1 Principles
An RLC header and a MAC header are added to data packets transmitted at the
RLC layer on the UE side. When estimating the amount of data to be scheduled
for a UE, the scheduler determines the overheads of the RLC header and MAC
header based on the value of the CellUlschAlgo.HeadOverheadForUlSch
parameter.
For any given MCS, the spectral efficiency achieved when a small number of RBs
are allocated is lower than that achieved when a large number of RBs are
allocated. When the UlSmallRBSpectralEffOptSw option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter is selected, the scheduler changes the
number of RBs to be allocated if the number is small. This ensures that the TBS
can meet the requirements of data transmission as much as possible.
These two measures reduce the number of uplink RLC segments, lowering the
service transmission delay and PDCCH overheads.
## 5.6.2.2 Network Analysis
## 5.6.2.2.1 Benefits
The reduction in the number of uplink RLC segments lowers the service
transmission delay and PDCCH overheads.
## 5.6.2.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
After the uplink RLC segment reduction function is enabled, the Service Drop
Rate may slightly decrease.

5.6.3 Optimized Scheduling upon Full Uplink Power
## 5.6.3.1 Principles
After the scheduler-controlled power function controlled by the
SchedulerCtrlPowerSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter
is enabled, UEs in the cell center that are not transmitting at full power may be
allocated more RBs, which increases interference on neighboring cells will increase.
In addition, it is possible that there are still uplink RBs available in the cell but the
RBs cannot be utilized by the UEs that are transmitting at full power. This wastes
uplink RB resources.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.228

The optimized scheduling upon full uplink power function controlled by the
UlSchRbAllocOptSw option of the CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchExtSwitch parameter
enables the eNodeB to increase the number of PUSCH RBs only for UEs that are
transmitting at full power. Therefore, the power per RB is further reduced
compared with when only the scheduler-controlled power function is enabled. This
ensures full utilization of uplink resources to increase uplink UE throughput
without increasing the total interference power. Figure 5-4 illustrates the
principles for the optimized scheduling upon full uplink power function.
Figure 5-4 Principles for the optimized scheduling upon full uplink power function
## 5.6.3.2 Network Analysis
## 5.6.3.2.1 Benefits
Optimized scheduling upon full uplink power can be enabled when the following
conditions are met:
●  The PUSCH RB usage is less than 30%.
●  The average number of UEs scheduled in the uplink is less than four.
After the function is enabled, the average uplink user-perceived throughput
increases by 1% to 10%. When either of the preceding conditions is not met, the
function does not take effect. (Average uplink user-perceived throughput =
L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced)
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.229

## 5.6.3.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
●  If optimized scheduling upon uplink full power is enabled in a cell with a
PUSCH RB usage less than 30%, the PUSCH RB usage slightly increases and
the average uplink MCS index decreases. As a result, the uplink spectral
efficiency decreases.
●  If the UlPAMCSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchExtSwitch
parameter is selected, the uplink RBLER slightly increases after optimized
scheduling upon uplink full power is enabled.
## Function Impacts
RATFunction
## Name
## Function

5.7 RB Allocation for Uplink Scheduling
## 5.7.1 Uplink Frequency Selective Scheduling
## 5.7.1.1 Principles
Uplink RBs can be allocated using frequency selective or non-frequency-selective
scheduling.
In FDD, uplink frequency selective scheduling takes effect by default, and the
UlEnhancedFssSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter
specifies whether uplink enhanced frequency selective scheduling takes effect.
The CellUlschAlgo.
UlRbAllocationStrategy parameter specifies the RB allocation
mode for UEs in uplink non-frequency-selective scheduling.
●  If this parameter is set to FS_NONFS_ADAPTIVE, the scheduler allocates RBs
to UEs from the high- to the low-frequency end in sequence.
●  If this parameter is set to FS_INRANDOM_ADAPTIVE, the scheduler allocates
RBs following the principles described in
## ICIC.
The scheduler considers the differences in channel quality for UEs in uplink
frequency selective scheduling, bringing frequency selective scheduling gains. The
eNodeB determines a sliding window size based on the number of RBs required
for each UE, and uses the sliding window to search for and select available
resources (candidate RBs shown in Figure 5-5) that can produce the most gains.
The eNodeB always starts the search from the low- to the high-frequency end.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.232

Figure 5-5 Resource allocation in uplink frequency selective scheduling
In FDD, if the UlFssWindSearchDirRandSw option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchExtSwitch parameter is selected, the scheduler can adjust
the resource search direction of the sliding window.
●  The direction used starts from the high- to the low-frequency end for cells
whose PCI modulo 2 is 0.
●  The direction used starts from the low- to the high-frequency end for cells
whose PCI modulo 2 is 1.
SRS is generally not configured for FDD cells. In this case, interference-based
frequency selective scheduling can be enabled by selecting the UlInterfFssSwitch
option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter. In addition, the
CellUlschAlgo.
UlInBasedFssSinrThld parameter on the eNodeB can be used to
specify the SINR threshold for interference-based uplink frequency-selective
scheduling. Only if the SINR of a UE is below this parameter value, the eNodeB
performs interference-based frequency selective scheduling for this UE.
## 5.7.1.2 Network Analysis
## 5.7.1.2.1 Benefits
Uplink frequency selective scheduling can reduce uplink interference and increase
the MCS indexes selected for uplink transmission, increasing uplink spectral
efficiency.
## 5.7.1.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
In FDD, the related functions have the following network impacts:
●  After the UlFssWindSearchDirRandSw option is selected, the average
perceived uplink throughput of UEs for which SRS resources have not been
configured increases.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.233

●  If SRS transmission is disabled in a cell, interference-based uplink frequency
selective scheduling reduces the uplink RBLER and therefore increases average
user-perceived uplink throughput.

parameter configurations for related functions based on the impact relationships
between the functions, as well as the actual network scenario.
//Enabling uplink enhanced frequency selective scheduling
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchSwitch=UlEnhancedFssSwitch-1;
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, UlRbAllocationStrategy=FS_INRANDOM_ADAPTIVE;

## Deactivation Command Examples
The following provides only deactivation command examples. You can determine
whether to restore the settings of other parameters based on actual network
conditions.
//Disabling interference-based uplink frequency selective scheduling
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchSwitch=UlInterfFssSwitch-0;
//Deselecting the UlFssWindSearchDirRandSw option
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchExtSwitch=UlFssWindSearchDirRandSw-0;
//Disabling uplink enhanced frequency selective scheduling
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchSwitch=UlEnhancedFssSwitch-0;

5.7.2 UL Multi-Cluster (FDD)
## 5.7.2.1 Principles
When the uplink PRBs allocated by the eNodeB to a single UE must be contiguous,
uplink PRB resources cannot be fully utilized because there remain multiple non-
contiguous PRB segments that cannot be allocated to the same UE in the event of
multi-UE scheduling or RB blocking.
UL Multi-Cluster was introduced to 3GPP TS 36.213 in Release 10. This function is
enabled when the UlMultiClusterSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchExtSwitch parameter is selected. With this function, the
uplink scheduler can allocate a maximum of two non-contiguous PRB clusters to a
single UE. Each cluster consists of an integer multiple of RBGs that are contiguous.
Figure 5-6 illustrates the principles for UL Multi-Cluster.
Figure 5-6 Principles for UL Multi-Cluster
This type of resource allocation takes effect for a UE when all the following
conditions are met:
●  The UE supports UL Multi-Cluster and is not in the single user multiple-input
multiple-output (SU-MIMO) state.
●  The maximum number of contiguous idle PRBs in the uplink band does not
meet the UE requirements.
●  There is sufficient power headroom at the UE. This requirement is set to
prevent the impact of maximum power reduction (MPR) on the UE transmit
power.
## 5.7.2.2 Network Analysis
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.238

## 5.7.2.2.1 Benefits
The UL Multi-Cluster function can be used in scenarios where the uplink spectrum
is fragmented, for example, when flexible PUCCH configuration is enabled or some
RBs are manually blocked. This function is not suitable when uplink interference is
distributed in an extremely unbalanced manner and there are large uplink
interference differences between separate idle RB segments. Therefore, this
function requires that the maximum interference difference between idle PUSCH
RBs be less than 10 dB. The UL Multi-Cluster function increases the uplink RB
usage and therefore the uplink peak throughput of UEs.

## 5.7.2.4.4 Activation Verification
Perform the following actions to verify the UL Multi-Cluster function.
●  Check whether UEs support the UL Multi-Cluster function.
During initial access, a UE uses the UECapabilityInformation message to
report its UL Multi-Cluster capabilities. If both of the multiClusterPUSCH-
WithinCC-r10 and nonContiguousUL-RA-WithinCC-Info-r10 information
elements (IEs) in this message have the value of "supported", the UE supports
UL Multi-Cluster. The items in the nonContiguousUL-RA-WithinCC-Info-r10 IE
have a one-to-one relationship with the items in the supportedBandListEUTRA
## IE.
●  Verify the activation of the UL Multi-Cluster function.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.242

a.  Run the MOD CELLALGOSWITCH command to disable the UL Multi-
Cluster function.
b.  On the MAE-Access, start cell PRB usage tracing (corresponding to Usage
of RB Monitoring on the GUI), and open the monitoring window.
c.  Enable a single UE to access the cell at the cell center and start a full-
buffer uplink service on the UE when the uplink spectrum is fragmented,
for example, when flexible PUCCH configuration or manual blocking of
certain PRBs is in use.
d.  Observe and record the real-time uplink PRB usage of the cell. If there
are two or more uplink spectrum fragments, only one of them can be
allocated to the UE.
e.  Run the MOD CELLALGOSWITCH command to enable the UL Multi-
Cluster function.
f.   Enable the UE to re-access the cell and start a full-buffer service on the
## UE.
g.  Observe and record the real-time uplink PRB usage of the cell. If there
are two or more uplink spectrum fragments, two of them can now be
allocated to the UE. If a greater number of PRBs are used in the uplink
than when the UL Multi-Cluster function is disabled, this function has
taken effect. Otherwise, the function has not taken effect.
## 5.7.2.4.5 Network Monitoring
Use the following counters to evaluate PUSCH RB interference:
L.UL.Interference.Avg.PRB0 to L.UL.Interference.Avg.PRB99
5.8 MCS Selection for Uplink Scheduling
The SINR of a UE reflects the uplink channel quality of the UE. eNodeBs select
MCSs for uplink scheduling based on SINRs. There are three steps involved in
selecting MCSs for uplink scheduling: SINR adjustment, initial MCS selection, and
MCS adjustment, as shown in Figure 5-7.
Figure 5-7 Process of determining MCSs for uplink scheduling
Uplink SINRs are adjusted to more accurately reflect real-time channel quality. The
adjusted uplink SINRs are used for subsequent initial MCS selection. The
adjustment helps uplink IBLERs to converge on the target values. For details on
uplink target IBLER configuration, see 5.8.2 Uplink Target IBLER Setting Policies.
On each UE scheduling occasion, initial MCS selection is performed to select an
appropriate MCS index, mainly based on comparison between the eNodeB
demodulation performance and the adjusted result of the measured SINR on the
UE bandwidth. To ensure the initial access performance, Huawei eNodeBs use a
fixed small-index MCS for Msg3 transmission during UE initial access.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.243

After the initial MCS has been selected, the eNodeB adjusts the selected MCS
based on related information. For details on MCS adjustment, see 5.8.5 MCS
## Adjustment.
5.8.1 Uplink AMC
## 5.8.1.1 Principles
Adaptive modulation and coding (AMC) is a basic procedure used during
scheduling. In this procedure, the eNodeB selects appropriate MCSs for UEs based
on current channel quality.

5.8.2 Uplink Target IBLER Setting Policies
## 5.8.2.1 Principles
For a moving UE, the SINR at the measurement time may be significantly different
from that at the scheduling time due to the impact of channel fading on signals. If
the SINR at the scheduling time cannot indicate the channel quality at the
scheduling time, the uplink IBLER does not converge on the target IBLER. To make
the uplink IBLER converge on the target IBLER, the eNodeB needs to determine
how much the measured SINR deviates from the actual channel quality based on
HARQ feedback to uplink data and adjusts the SINR accordingly.
The uplink target IBLER can be a fixed or adaptive value. Table 5-39 lists the
uplink target IBLER setting policies for different types of services.
Table 5-39 Uplink target IBLER setting policies
## Service Type
Fixed Uplink IBLERAdaptive Uplink IBLER
VoLTE servicesSupportedNot supported.
Data serviceSupportedSupported

adaptation is used.
For details, see
Uplink Target IBLER

details, see 5.8.3

Fixed Uplink Target IBLER Setting
The conditions for different types of services to support fixed uplink target IBLER
setting are as follows:
●  VoLTE services only support fixed uplink target IBLER setting.
●  Data services support fixed uplink target IBLER setting only if the
UlIblerAdjustSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter
and the UlPAMCSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchExtSwitch
parameter are deselected.
A fixed uplink target IBLER can be set at the bearer level in addition to the cell
level:
●  If all the CellQciPara.
SinrAdjustTargetIbler parameter values corresponding
to the bearers set up for a UE are 0, the uplink target IBLER is equal to the
CellUlschAlgo.SinrAdjustTargetIbler parameter value.
●  If one of the CellQciPara.
SinrAdjustTargetIbler parameter values
corresponding to the bearers set up for a UE is not 0, this non-zero value is
used as the uplink target IBLER.
If more than one of the CellQciPara.
SinrAdjustTargetIbler parameter values
corresponding to the bearers set up for a UE is not 0, the non-zero parameter
value with the QCI that takes precedence over the other QCIs is used as the
uplink target IBLER for the UE. The QCIs in descending order of precedence
are as follows: QCI 2, QCIs for VoLTE and PTT services, QCIs for non-IMS
signaling, and QCIs for IMS signaling.
If there are multiple QCIs for non-IMS signaling, these QCIs are sorted in
descending order of QCI priority. If multiple QCIs for non-IMS signaling have
the same QCI priority but correspond to different uplink target IBLER values,
the smallest value is used.
Uplink Target IBLER Adaptation
The UlIblerAdjustSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchSwitch parameter
determines whether to enable uplink target IBLER adaptation. When the
UlIblerAdjustSwitch option is selected, the uplink target IBLER adapts to the rules
listed in Table 5-40.
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.246

Table 5-40 Uplink target IBLER adaptation rules
Option SettingAdaptation Rule
The SinrFlunIBlerAdaptSwitch
option of the
CellUlschAlgo.UlTargetIBlerAdap
tType parameter is selected.
SINR-fluctuation-based uplink IBLER
adaptation adaptively changes the uplink
target IBLER to 5%, 10%, or 30%,
depending on the UE's distance to the
eNodeB and the uplink SINR fluctuation.
● If the SINR fluctuation is significant or
the UE is far away from the eNodeB, a
large target IBLER value will be used.
● If the UE is close to the eNodeB and
the SINR is stable, a small target IBLER
value will be used.
The SinrJumpIBlerAdaptSwitch
option of the
CellUlschAlgo.UlTargetIBlerAdap
tType parameter is selected.
If there is a sudden and transient SINR
change (for 1 to 3 TTIs), the target IBLER
value is 100% if the SINR change is great
or 50% if the SINR change is at a medium
level or slightly above. This function is
supported only by FDD.
## The
CellUlschAlgo.UlIBlerAdaptBig-
TrafficSw parameter is set to ON.
If uplink large-packet data transmission
lasts for more than 1s on only one UE in
the cell, the target IBLER adaptation
function takes effect only for this UE.

throughput. (Average uplink user-perceived throughput =
L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced)

Before using MML commands, confirm the related parameter modification

## Activation Command Examples
Before using MML commands, refer to 5.8.2.2.2 Impacts and 5.8.2.3.2 Software
and complete the parameter configurations for related functions based on the
impact and mutually exclusive relationships between the functions, as well as the
actual network scenario.
//Enabling uplink target IBLER adaptation
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchSwitch=UlIblerAdjustSwitch-1;
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0,
UlTargetIBlerAdaptType=SinrFlunIBlerAdaptSwitch-1&SinrJumpIBlerAdaptSwitch-1,
UlIBlerAdaptBigTrafficSw=ON;
## Optimization Command Examples
//Setting the cell-specific target IBLER
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, SinrAdjustTargetIbler=10;
//Setting the QCI-specific target IBLER, which is used to replace the cell-specific target IBLER
MOD CELLQCIPARA: LocalCellId=0, Qci=9, SinrAdjustTargetIbler=10;

## 5.8.2.4.5 Network Monitoring
Check the uplink IBLER, which is calculated using the following formula: Uplink
IBLER = (L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.ErrTB.Ibler +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.ErrTB.Ibler + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.ErrTB.Ibler)/
(L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.TB + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.TB)
## 5.8.3 PAMC
## 5.8.3.1 Principles
UEs that have just accessed a network use a fixed initial SINR adjustment value,
and therefore the adjusted SINR does not immediately approach the expected
value if the UEs are scheduled infrequently because of low traffic volume. This
slow change in the adjusted SINR results in a failure to accurately indicate uplink
channel quality, affecting uplink UE throughput.
PAMC, controlled by the UlPAMCSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchExtSwitch parameter, uses AMC to maximize uplink
perceived throughput. This function introduces a cell-level SINR adjustment value.
For each UE that has just accessed a network, the eNodeB now obtains a more
accurate initial UE-level SINR adjustment value for subsequent initial MCS
selection. eNodeBs consider the number of scheduled PRBs and SINR fluctuation
in addition to the adjusted SINR for more accurate initial MCS selection, as shown
in Figure 5-8.
Figure 5-8 Process of PAMC
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.251

After PAMC is enabled, the uplink target IBLER for UEs in the cell center is
decreased. As a result, the percentage of times 64QAM is selected for these UEs
decreases and the percentage of times 16QAM is selected for these UEs increases.
In contrast, the uplink target IBLER for UEs at the cell edge is increased after
PAMC is enabled. As a result, the percentage of times QPSK is selected for these
UEs decreases and the percentage of times 16QAM is selected for these UEs
increases.
## NO TE
●  PAMC cannot be used with UEs enabled with TM2 in SU-MIMO, TTI bundling, VoLTE, V-
MIMO, or PTT.
●  PAMC does not take effect in cells whose Cell.
UlCyclicPrefix is set to
EXTENDED_CP(Extended).
●  For UEs that have accessed a cell before PAMC is enabled in this cell, PAMC takes effect
on initial MCS selection for these UEs only after they re-access the cell.
## 5.8.3.2 Network Analysis
## 5.8.3.2.1 Benefits
The PAMC function causes the uplink IBLER to increase by 0%–40%, but it lifts
average uplink user-perceived throughput by 5%–15%.
●  Uplink IBLER = (L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.ErrTB.Ibler +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.ErrTB.Ibler + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.ErrTB.Ibler +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.ErrTB.Ibler)/(L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.TB + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.TB)
●  Average uplink user-perceived throughput = L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/
L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced

5.8.4 Data-Learning-based Uplink Intelligent AMC
## 5.8.4.1 Principles
During uplink scheduling, after the eNodeB delivers an uplink grant to a UE, the
UE sends uplink data several TTIs later. This means that the MCS index indicated
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.255

in the uplink grant delivered by the eNodeB may not accurately match the uplink
channel quality during uplink data transmission, affecting the uplink UE
throughput.
Data-learning-based uplink intelligent AMC collects a large amount of UE
scheduling information, and uses the collected data to train a neural network
model. The collected information includes the uplink SINR at the time of data
transmission, uplink SINR measured in several historical channel measurements,
scheduling interval, and allocated RB positions. The trained model allows the
eNodeB to predict the SINR at the uplink transmission moment when delivering
the uplink grant, thereby guiding the selection of the optimal uplink MCS index,
improving the transmission spectral efficiency of UEs, and increasing the uplink UE
throughput. This function is controlled by the UL_INTEL_AMC_SELECTION_SW
option of the CellIntelAmcConfig.
IntelligentAmcSwitch parameter. In addition,
Cell.WorkMode must be set to UL_DL.
After a model is built, periodic monitoring of the model is started. If the difference
between the predicted uplink SINR and the actual uplink SINR exceeds the
CellIntelAmcConfig.
UlPredErrorUpperLimit parameter value, data is collected
again for model update. Figure 5-9 shows the model building process.
Figure 5-9 Uplink SINR prediction model building process
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.256

Model building requires a large amount of memory on the main control board.
The option configuration requirements for the
eNodeBResModeAlgo.ServiceMode parameter vary depending on the main
control board:
●  UMPTb: The UMPTB_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH option of the
eNodeBResModeAlgo.
ServiceMode parameter must be selected.
●  UMPTe/UMPTga: The AI_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH or
AI_ENHANCEMENT_OPT_SWITCH option of the
eNodeBResModeAlgo.ServiceMode parameter must be selected. These two
options cannot be both selected.
●  UMPTg: The UMPTG_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH option of the
eNodeBResModeAlgo.
ServiceMode parameter must be selected.
The model building duration may vary slightly between main control boards.
## 5.8.4.2 Network Analysis
## 5.8.4.2.1 Benefits
It is good practice to enable uplink intelligent AMC when all of the following
conditions are met:
●  UEs are distributed near the cell center, at the cell edge, and at a medium
distance from the cell center. The proportion of UEs whose path loss falls
within the range of [105, 135] exceeds 50%.
●  The proportion of small-packet bits is 30–80%.
●  The uplink PRB usage is greater than 10%.
Among them:
●  UEs whose path loss falls within each range are measured by
L.Traffic.User.PL0 to L.Traffic.User.PL14. The numbers of UEs whose path loss
falls within the range of [105, 135] are measured by L.Traffic.User.PL4 to
L.Traffic.User.PL9.
●  Proportion of small-packet bits = L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.SmallPkt/L.Thrp.bits.UL
●  Uplink PRB usage = L.ChMeas.PRB.UL.Used.Avg/L.ChMeas.PRB.UL.Avail
Under the preceding conditions, uplink intelligent AMC increases the User Uplink
Average Throughput by 5–10%. As the uplink load increases, the speed of model
building slows down, and the gains that can be obtained in the uplink may be
affected.
## 5.8.4.2.2 Impacts
## Network Impacts
After uplink intelligent AMC is enabled, the CPU usage (indicated by
VS.BBUBoard.CPULoad.Mean and VS.BBUBoard.CPULoad.Max) may increase.
The average uplink MCS index and the uplink IBLER may change depending on UE
distribution and the proportion of bits of small packets. The change in the uplink
IBLER may also lead to the change in the uplink RBLER. Note the following:
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.257

●  Average uplink MCS index = (L.ChMeas.PUSCH.MCS.0 x 0 +
L.ChMeas.PUSCH.MCS.1 x 1 + ... + L.ChMeas.PUSCH.MCS.28 x 28)/
Sum(L.ChMeas.PUSCH.MCS.0 ... L.ChMeas.PUSCH.MCS.28)
●  Uplink IBLER = (L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.ErrTB.Ibler +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.ErrTB.Ibler + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.ErrTB.Ibler +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.ErrTB.Ibler)/(L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.TB + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.TB)
●  Uplink RBLER = (L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.ErrTB.Rbler +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.ErrTB.Rbler + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.ErrTB.Rbler
+ L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.ErrTB.Rbler)/(L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.TB + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.TB)

5.8.4.4.2 Using MML Commands
Before using MML commands, refer to 5.8.4.2.2 Impacts and 5.8.4.3.2 Software
and complete the parameter configurations for related functions based on the
impact and mutually exclusive relationships between the functions, as well as the
actual network scenario.
Before using MML commands, confirm the related parameter modification
precautions by referring to the fields including "Service Interrupted After
Modification" and "Caution" for the corresponding parameters in the parameter
reference.
## NO TE
To enable or disable the main control board enhancement mode:
●  If the main control board model has been determined, you are advised to select or
deselect the corresponding option.
●  If the main control board model is unknown, you are advised to select or deselect all the
AI_ENHANCEMENT_OPT_SWITCH, UMPTB_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH, and
UMPTG_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH options. The base station can automatically identify
the main control board model and activates or deactivates the enhancement mode of
only the identified main control board model.
## Activation Command Examples
//Enabling uplink intelligent AMC
MOD CELLINTELAMCCONFIG: LocalCellId=0, IntelligentAmcSwitch=UL_INTEL_AMC_SELECTION_SW-1;
eRAN
## Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Uplink Scheduling
Issue 11 (2024-06-21)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.264

//Selecting options of ServiceMode
//Selecting the AI_ENHANCEMENT_OPT_SWITCH option if the UMPTe or UMPTga is used as the main
control board, and smart carrier selection based on virtual grids is enabled or the main control board works
in LN or GULN mode
## MOD ENODEBRESMODEALGO:
ServiceMode=AI_ENHANCEMENT_OPT_SWITCH-1&AI_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH-0;
//Selecting the AI_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH option if the UMPTe or UMPTga is used as the main control
board, smart carrier selection based on virtual grids is disabled, and the main control board does not work
in LN or GULN mode
## MOD ENODEBRESMODEALGO:
ServiceMode=AI_ENHANCEMENT_OPT_SWITCH-0&AI_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH-1;
//Selecting the UMPTG_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH option if the UMPTg is used as the main control board
MOD ENODEBRESMODEALGO: ServiceMode=UMPTG_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH-1;
//Selecting the UMPTB_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH option if the UMPTb is used as the main control board
MOD ENODEBRESMODEALGO: ServiceMode=UMPTB_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH-1;
//Restarting the eNodeB application (RST APP is a high-risk command. Before running this command, read
the precautions in
3900 & 5900 Series Base Station MML Command Reference.)
## RST APP: AID=1;
## Optimization Command Examples
//Configuring the upper limit on the error of the prediction for the uplink
MOD CELLINTELAMCCONFIG: LocalCellId=0, UlPredErrorUpperLimit=20;
## Deactivation Command Examples
The following provides only deactivation command examples. You can determine
whether to restore the settings of other parameters based on actual network
conditions.
//Disabling uplink intelligent AMC
MOD CELLINTELAMCCONFIG: LocalCellId=0, IntelligentAmcSwitch=UL_INTEL_AMC_SELECTION_SW-0;
//Deselecting options of ServiceMode
//Deselecting the AI_ENHANCEMENT_OPT_SWITCH option if the UMPTe or UMPTga is used as the main
control board, and smart carrier selection based on virtual grids is enabled or the main control board works
in LN or GULN mode
MOD ENODEBRESMODEALGO: ServiceMode=AI_ENHANCEMENT_OPT_SWITCH-0;
//Deselecting the AI_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH option if the UMPTe or UMPTga is used as the main control
board, smart carrier selection based on virtual grids is disabled, and the main control board does not work
in LN or GULN mode
MOD ENODEBRESMODEALGO: ServiceMode=AI_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH-0;
//Deselecting the UMPTG_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH option if the UMPTg is used as the main control board
MOD ENODEBRESMODEALGO: ServiceMode=UMPTG_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH-0;
//Deselecting the UMPTB_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH option if the UMPTb is used as the main control board
MOD ENODEBRESMODEALGO: ServiceMode=UMPTB_ENHANCEMENT_SWITCH-0;
//Restarting the eNodeB application (RST APP is a high-risk command. Before running this command, read
the precautions in
3900 & 5900 Series Base Station MML Command Reference.)
## RST APP: AID=1;

5.8.5 MCS Adjustment
## 5.8.5.1 Principles
After initial MCS selection, the eNodeB can adjust the MCS based on the cell-
specific SRS subframe configuration, uplink control information on the PUSCH, and
UE capabilities. The adjustment procedure is as follows:
-  If cell-specific SRS or uplink control information on the PUSCH needs to be
transmitted when RBs are scheduled for uplink data transmission of a UE, the
eNodeB needs to adjust the MCS.
The symbols in which cell-specific SRS is transmitted are unavailable for
PUSCH transmission and uplink control information transmission on the
PUSCH reduces the resources for data transmission, resulting in a higher
PUSCH code rate and accordingly a higher IBLER for data transmission. The
MCS must be adjusted accordingly to ensure that the scheduled data is
correctly demodulated.
Uplink control information transmitted on the PUSCH includes ACKs, RIs, and
CQIs. An MCS for uplink control information is selected based on the result of
the MCS used for data transmission minus an index offset. A higher index
offset indicates a higher transmission reliability for uplink control information
on the PUSCH, but more resources are used by the uplink control information.
If the uplink control information on the PUSCH has a high bit error rate (BER)
in adverse radio conditions, higher index offsets can be specified. The index
offsets for ACKs, RIs, and CQIs transmitted on the PUSCH are specified by the
cell-level parameters CellUciOnPuschPara.DeltaOffsetAckIndex,
CellUciOnPuschPara.DeltaOffsetRiIndex, and
CellUciOnPuschPara.DeltaOffsetCqiIndex, respectively.
-  UE capabilities must also be considered because the highest-order MCS
supported may vary for different UEs.

## 6 Parameters
The following hyperlinked EXCEL files of parameter documents match the
software version with which this document is released.
●  Node Parameter Reference: contains device and transport parameters.
●  eNodeBFunction Parameter Reference: contains all parameters related to
radio access functions, including air interface management, access control,
mobility control, and radio resource management.
●  eNodeBFunction Used Reserved Parameter List: contains the reserved
parameters that are in use and those that have been disused.
## NO TE
You can find the EXCEL files of parameter reference and used reserved parameter list for
the software version used on the live network from the product documentation delivered
with that version.
FAQ 1: How do I find the parameters related to a certain feature from
parameter reference?
Step 1Open the EXCEL file of parameter reference.
Step 2On the Parameter List sheet, filter the Feature ID column. Click Text Filters and
choose Contains. Enter the feature ID, for example, LOFD-001016 or
## TDLOFD-001016.
Step 3Click OK. All parameters related to the feature are displayed.
----End
FAQ 2: How do I find the information about a certain reserved parameter
from the used reserved parameter list?
Step 1Open the EXCEL file of the used reserved parameter list.
Step 2On the Used Reserved Parameter List sheet, use the MO, Parameter ID, and BIT
columns to locate the reserved parameter, which may be only a bit of a parameter.
View its information, including the meaning, values, impacts, and product version
in which it is activated for use.
----End
